On generalized main conjectures and p p -adic Stark conjectures for Artin motives
Alexandre Maksoud

Abstract
. Given an odd prime number p p and a p p -stabilized Artin representation ρ \rho over ℚ \mathbb{Q} , we introduce a family of p p -adic Stark regulators and we formulate an Iwasawa-Greenberg main conjecture and a p p -adic Stark conjecture which can be seen as an explicit strengthening of conjectures by Perrin-Riou and Benois in the context of Artin motives. We show that these conjectures imply the p p -part of the Tamagawa number conjecture for Artin motives at s = 0 s=0 and we obtain unconditional results on the torsionness of Selmer groups. We also relate our new conjectures with various main conjectures and variants of p p -adic Stark conjectures that appear in the literature. In the case of monomial representations, we prove that our conjectures are essentially equivalent to some newly introduced Iwasawa-theoretic conjectures for Rubin-Stark elements. We derive from this a p p -adic Beilinson-Stark formula for finite-order characters of an imaginary quadratic field in which p p is inert. Along the way, we prove that the Gross-Kuz’min conjecture unconditionally holds for abelian extensions of imaginary quadratic fields.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
11R23 (primary), 11R29, 11R42 (secondary).

1.
Introduction

Iwasawa theory traditionally focuses on the construction of
p
-adic
L
-functions and on their relation with arithmetic invariants of number fields. Concurrently with the first major achievements of the theory
[
MW84
,
Wil90
,
Rub91
]
, several attempts were made in order to define the conjectural
p
-adic
L
-function of a motive
[
CPR89
,
Coa91
,
Gre94
]
and its corresponding Selmer group
[
Gre89
,
Gre91
]
. The motive in question was assumed to admit a critical value in the sense of Deligne and to be ordinary at
p
, hypotheses which were relaxed in a work of Perrin-Riou
[
PR95
]
using her extended logarithm map. In
[
Ben14a
]
, Benois proposed later a conjectural description of the behavior of Perrin-Riou’s
p
-adic
L
-function at “trivial zeros” via Nekovář’s theory of Selmer complexes
[
Nek06
]
.

While being ordinary at unramified primes, motives coming from Artin representations are seldom critical and the arithmetic of their
L
-values at
s=0
is described by “refined Stark conjectures”, thanks to the work of various mathematicians beginning with Stark’s and Rubin’s influential papers
[
Sta75
,
Sta80
,
Rub96
]
and culminating in
[
Bur11
]
. In the particular case of monomial Artin motives, that is, when the associated Artin representation is induced from a one-dimensional character, a long-term strategy to tackle these conjectures with the aid of Iwasawa theory was presented in
[
BKS17
]
. A general main conjecture (called “higher rank Iwasawa main conjecture”) and an extra zero conjecture (called “Iwasawa-theoretic Mazur-Rubin-Sano conjecture”) are formulated in terms of Rubin-Stark elements, but neither a
p
-adic
L
-function nor an
\mathcal{L}
-invariant play a part in this work. Nevertheless, the authors verify that their extra zero conjecture generalizes the Gross-Stark conjecture. Not long after that, Büyükboduk and Sakamoto in
[
BS22
]
used Coleman maps to deduce from the Mazur-Rubin-Sano conjecture a
p
-adic Beilinson formula for Katz’s
p
-adic
L
-function.

The aim of our paper is to provide a unifying approach to the cyclotomic Iwasawa theory for Artin motives which is close in spirit to Perrin-Riou’s and Benois’ treatment and which generalizes many aspects of
[
BKS17
,
BS22
]
. Not only does this encompass classical conjectures on Deligne-Ribet’s and Katz’s
p
-adic
L
-functions, but it also sheds light on recent constructions of
p
-adic
L
-functions attached to a classical weight one modular cuspform
f
in
[
BD16
,
Mak21
]
and on new variants of the Gross-Stark conjecture for the adjoint motive of
f
in
[
DLR16
]
. More specifically, one of the central objects in this paper is the Selmer group
X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
whose properties are studied by Greenberg and Vatsal in
[
GV20
]
and by the author in
[
Mak21
]
. It depends on the choice of an ordinary
p
-stabilization
\rho^{+}
of the
p
-adic realization
\rho
of the Artin motive. Although there is no canonical choice for
\rho^{+}
when
\rho
is non-critical, a fruitful idea in this work is to think of
\rho^{+}
as an
additional parameter
.

The question of the torsionness of Selmer groups over the Iwasawa algebra is a recurring theme in Iwasawa theory. When
\rho
is an Artin representation, it was shown in
[
Mak21
]
that delicate conjectures coming from
p
-adic transcendence theory arise through the study of
X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
(see also
[
GV20
]
). A first crucial aspect of this work is to obtain, under an unramifiedness assumption at
p
, finer information on the structure of
X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
with the aid of modified Coleman maps and of classical freeness results on
{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
-towers of global units. We also relate its characteristic ideal with Perrin-Riou’s module of
p
-adic
L
-functions for the dual motive associated with
\rho
.

On the analytic side, our conjectural
p
-adic
L
-function interpolates the algebraic part of Artin
L
-values at
s=0
given by a recipe of Stark
[
Sta75
,
Tat84
]
. “Extra zeros” in the sense of Benois abound in this setting, and we also formulate an extra zero conjecture which is compatible with the one in
[
Ben14a
]
. It involves a new
\mathcal{L}
-invariant which computes Benois’
\mathcal{L}
-invariant when the Artin motive is crystalline at
p
, and which also recovers various
\mathcal{L}
-invariants that appear in the literature
[
Gro81
,
RR21
,
RRV21
,
BS22
]
.

In the next section we formulate our principal conjecture.

1.1.
The conjecture

We fix once and for all embeddings
\iota_{\infty}:\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
and
\iota_{\ell}:\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\hookrightarrow\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\ell}
for all primes
\ell
. Let

\rho:G_{\mathbb{Q}}\longrightarrow\GL_{E}(W)

be a
d
-dimensional representation of
G_{\mathbb{Q}}=\Gal(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu/\mathbb{Q})
of finite image with coefficients in a number field
E\subseteq\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
. We always assume that
W
does not contain the trivial representation,
i.e.
, one has
\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{Q},W)=0
. For any character
\eta:G_{\mathbb{Q}}\longrightarrow\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\times}
of finite order, we denote by
W_{\eta}
the underlying space of
\rho\otimes\eta
, by
E_{\eta}\subseteq\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
its coefficient field and by
H_{\eta}\subseteq\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
the Galois extension cut out by
\rho\otimes\eta
. Our fixed embedding
\iota_{\infty}
allows us to see
\rho\otimes\eta
as an Artin representation, and we let
L(\rho\otimes\eta,s)
be its Artin
L
-function. It is a meromorphic function over
\mathbb{C}
, and it is known that
L(\rho\otimes\eta,1)\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
provided that
\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{Q},W_{\eta})=0
. Assuming further that
\eta
is even, the functional equation implies that
L((\rho\otimes\eta)^{*},s)
has a zero of order
d^{+}=\dim\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{R},W)
at
s=0
, where
(-)^{*}
stands for the contragredient representation. The non-abelian Stark conjecture
[
Sta75
]
describes the transcendental part of its leading term
L^{*}((\rho\otimes\eta)^{*},0)
at
s=0
as follows. Set

\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}((\rho\otimes\eta)^{*}(1))=\Hom_{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}(W_{\eta},E_{\eta}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{O}_{H_{\eta}}^{\times}),

where
\mathcal{O}_{H_{\eta}}^{\times}
is the group of units of
H_{\eta}
. It is a
E_{\eta}
-vector space of dimension
d^{+}
by Dirichlet’s unit theorem, and it provides a natural rational structure for a certain global Bloch-Kato Selmer group (see (
11
)).
There is a natural
\mathbb{C}
-linear perfect pairing

(1)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rclcc}\mathbb{C}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{R},W)&\times&\mathbb{C}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}((\rho\otimes\eta)^{*}(1))&\longrightarrow&\mathbb{C}\\
(1\otimes w&,&1\otimes\psi)&\mapsto&\log_{\infty}(\psi(w)),\end{array}\right.

where
\log_{\infty}:\mathbb{C}\otimes\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\times}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
is given by
x\otimes a\mapsto-x\log|\iota_{\infty}(a)|
(and
\log
refers to the usual real logarithm). Stark’s regulator attached to
\rho\otimes\eta
is the determinant
\Reg_{\omega_{\infty}^{+}}(\rho\otimes\eta)\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
of the pairing (
1
) with respect to rational bases
\omega_{\infty}^{+}\in\det_{E}\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{R},W)
and
\omega_{\mathrm{f},\eta}\in\det_{E_{\eta}}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}((\rho\otimes\eta)^{*}(1))
, and the non-abelian Stark conjecture implies

(2)

\dfrac{L^{*}((\rho\otimes\eta)^{*},0)}{\Reg_{\omega_{\infty}^{+}}(\rho\otimes\eta)}\in E_{\eta}^{\times},\qquad\textrm{or, equivalently,}\qquad\frac{L(\rho\otimes\eta,1)}{(i\pi)^{d^{-}}\Reg_{\omega_{\infty}^{+}}(\rho\otimes\eta)}\in E_{\eta}^{\times},

where
d^{-}=d-d^{+}
(see Lemma
3.4.2
).

Fix once and for all a prime number
p
as well as an isomorphism

j:\mathbb{C}\simeq\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}

such that
\iota_{p}=j\circ\iota_{\infty}
. Letting
E_{p}
be the completion of
\iota_{p}(E)
inside
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}
, one may see
\rho
as a
p
-adic representation by putting
W_{p}=W\otimes_{E,\iota_{p}}E_{p}
. We call a
p
-stabilization
of
W_{p}
any
G_{{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
-stable linear subspace
W_{p}^{+}\subseteq W_{p}
of dimension
d^{+}
, where
G_{{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
is the local Galois group
\Gal(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}/{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
. Any
p
-stabilization
(W_{p}^{+},\rho^{+})
yields a
p
-adic analogue of the complex pairing (
1
) by considering

(3)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rclcc}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}\otimes W_{p}^{+}&\times&\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}((\rho\otimes\eta)^{*}(1))&\longrightarrow&\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}\\
(1\otimes w&,&1\otimes\psi)&\mapsto&\log_{p}(\psi(w)),\end{array}\right.

where
\log_{p}:\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}\otimes\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\times}\longrightarrow\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}
is given by
x\otimes a\mapsto x\log_{p}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(\iota_{p}(a))
(and
\log_{p}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
is the Iwasawa logarithm). A
p
-stabilization
W_{p}^{+}
is called
\eta
-
admissible
if the
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}
-linear pairing (
3
) is perfect, and it is simply called
admissible
when
\eta
is the trivial character
\mathds{1}
(cf. Definition
3.1.1
).
The
p
-adic Stark regulator attached to
\rho\otimes\eta
is defined as the determinant
\Reg_{\omega_{p}^{+}}(\rho\otimes\eta)\in\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}
of the pairing (
3
) with respect to bases
\omega_{p}^{+}\in\det_{E_{p}}W_{p}^{+}
and
\omega_{\mathrm{f},\eta}\in\det_{E_{\eta}}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}((\rho\otimes\eta)^{*}(1))
. Note that
\Reg_{\omega_{p}^{+}}(\rho\otimes\eta)\in\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}
vanishes precisely when its
\eta
-admissibility fails.

A
p
-stabilization might
a priori
be
\eta
-admissible or not, but in the special case where
W_{p}^{+}
is
motivic
,
i.e.
, when it admits a
E
-rational structure (cf. Definition
3.1.1
), its
\eta
-admissibility follows from a theorem of Brumer when
d^{+}=1
and from the weak
p
-adic Schanuel conjecture in general (see §
3.1
).

Let
\mathcal{O}_{p}
be the ring of integers of
E_{p}
and fix a Galois-stable
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-lattice
T_{p}
of
W_{p}
. Then
T_{p}^{+}=W_{p}^{+}\bigcap T_{p}
is a lattice inside
W_{p}^{+}
. We always assume that
\omega_{\infty}^{+}
and
\omega_{p}^{+}
are
T_{p}
-
optimal
, that is, they are respective bases of
\det_{\mathcal{O}_{p}}\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{R},T_{p})
and
\det_{\mathcal{O}_{p}}T_{p}^{+}
.

Let
\Gamma
be the Galois group of the
{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
-cyclotomic extension
\mathbb{Q}_{\infty}=\cup_{n}\mathbb{Q}_{n}
of
\mathbb{Q}
and let
\widehat{\Gamma}\subseteq\Hom(\Gamma,\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\times})
be the set of
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
-valued characters of
\Gamma
of finite order. Via
\iota_{\infty}
, the elements of
\widehat{\Gamma}
correspond to (necessarily even) Dirichlet characters of
p
-power order and conductor, and via
\iota_{p}
they become
p
-adic characters which we see as homomorphisms of
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-algebras
\Lambda\longrightarrow\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}
, where
\Lambda=\mathcal{O}_{p}[[\Gamma]]
is the Iwasawa algebra. Consider the Greenberg-style Selmer group

X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+}):=\ker\left[\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\infty},D_{p})\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p,\infty}^{\mathrm{ur}},D_{p}^{-})\times\prod_{\ell\neq p}\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{\ell,\infty},D_{p})\right]^{\vee},

where
D_{p}
(resp.
D_{p}^{-}
) is the divisible
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-module
W_{p}/T_{p}
(resp.
D_{p}/(\im W_{p}^{+}\rightarrow D_{p})
),
\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{\ell,\infty}\subseteq\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\ell}
the maximal unramified extension of the completion of
\mathbb{Q}_{\infty}
along
\iota_{\ell}
and
(-)^{\vee}
the Pontryagin dual. Then
X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
has a structure of
\Lambda
-module of finite type and we denote by
\textrm{char}_{\Lambda}X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
its characteristic ideal. By convention,
\textrm{char}_{\Lambda}X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})=\{0\}
if
X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
is not torsion.

Conjecture A
.

Let
p
be an odd prime at which
\rho
is unramified. Fix a
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-stable
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-lattice
T_{p}
of
W_{p}
and a
p
-stabilization
(\rho^{+},W_{p}^{+})
of
W_{p}
. Pick any
T_{p}
-optimal bases
\omega_{p}^{+}
and
\omega_{\infty}^{+}
as before.

EX
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
:

There exists an element
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
in the field of fractions of
\Lambda
which has no pole outside the trivial character and which satisfies the following interpolation property: for all non-trivial characters
\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}
of exact conductor
p^{n}
, one has

\displaystyle\eta(\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}})

\displaystyle={\det(\rho^{-})(\sigma_{p}^{-n})}\ \Reg_{\omega_{p}^{+}}(\rho\otimes\eta)\cdot j\left(\frac{\tau(\eta)^{d^{-}}}{\tau(\rho\otimes\eta)}\ \dfrac{L^{*}\left((\rho\otimes\eta)^{*},0\right)}{\Reg_{\omega_{\infty}^{+}}(\rho\otimes\eta)}\right),

where
\tau(-)
is a Galois-Gauss sum (see §
3.3
),
\sigma_{p}
the arithmetic Frobenius at
p
, and
\det(\rho^{-})
the determinant of the local Galois representation acting on
W_{p}^{-}=W_{p}/W_{p}^{+}
.

IMC
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
:

The statement
EX
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
holds, and
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
is a generator of
\textrm{char}_{\Lambda}X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
.

EZC
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
:

Let
e
be the dimension of
W_{p}^{-,0}:=\mathrm{H}^{0}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}},W_{p}^{-})
and assume that
\rho^{+}
is admissible. Then
EX
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
holds,
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
vanishes at
\mathds{1}
with multiplicity at least
e
, and one has

\displaystyle\frac{1}{e!}\dfrac{\diff^{e}}{\diff\!s^{e}}\kappa^{s}(\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}})\bigg|_{s=0}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{e}\ \mathcal{E}(\rho,\rho^{+})\ \mathcal{L}(\rho,\rho^{+})\ \Reg_{\omega_{p}^{+}}(\rho)\cdot j\left(\tau(\rho)^{-1}\ \dfrac{L^{*}\left(\rho^{*},0\right)}{\Reg_{\omega_{\infty}^{+}}(\rho)}\right),

where
\kappa^{s}\in\Hom_{\mathcal{O}-\textrm{alg}}(\Lambda,\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p})
is the homomorphism induced by the character
\langle\chi_{\cyc}\rangle:\Gamma\simeq 1+p{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\subseteq\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}^{\times}
, raised to the power
s\in{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
,
\mathcal{L}(\rho,\rho^{+})\in\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}
is the
\mathcal{L}
-invariant defined in §
3.8
, and
\mathcal{E}(\rho,\rho^{+})
is a modified Euler factor given by

\mathcal{E}(\rho,\rho^{+})=\det(1-p^{-1}\sigma_{p}\big|W_{p}^{+})\det(1-\sigma_{p}^{-1}\big|W_{p}^{-}/W_{p}^{-,0}).

In other words, we conjecture that the special values
L^{*}((\rho\otimes\eta)^{*},0)
for
\eta
varying in
\widehat{\Gamma}
and suitably corrected by a complex and a
p
-adic Stark regulator can be
p
-adically interpolated by a
p
-adic measure which generates the characteristic ideal of
X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
over
\Lambda
.

The first two parts of Conjecture
A
should be thought as an explicit variant of Perrin-Riou’s main conjecture
[
PR95
,
Col00
]
for the arithmetic dual of
\rho
, while the last part is analogous to Benois’ extra zeros conjecture
[
Ben14a
]
(see §
3.9
). Conjecture
A
is strongly contingent on the nonabelian Stark conjecture.

The measure
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
is uniquely determined by the interpolation property
EX
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
and it also depends on the choice of
\iota_{\infty}
and
\iota_{p}
. The dependence of
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
on the bases
\omega_{\infty}^{+}
,
\omega_{p}^{+}
and
\omega_{\mathrm{f},\eta}
turns out to be very mild (see Proposition
3.4.3
).

Conjecture
A
is compatible with a
p
-adic Artin formalism (Remark
3.10.2
).
The statement of the conjecture makes sense even when
X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
is not of
\Lambda
-torsion, in which case Conjecture
A
follows from Theorem
A
below.

We illustrate in §
6
some special cases of Conjecture
A
and their relations with classical conjectures in the literature. When
d^{+}=0
, the construction of
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
is due to Greenberg and is based on the
p
-adic
L
-function of Deligne-Ribet and Cassou-Noguès. Wiles’ deep work on the main conjecture then implies
IMC
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
. The statement
EZC
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
when
d^{+}=0
is equivalent to the Gross-Stark conjecture for
\rho
, the
\mathcal{L}
-invariant being equal to Gross’ regulator (see §
6.3
). When
d^{+}=d
, both
EX
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
and
EZC
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
are implied by a conjecture of Tate on Deligne-Ribet’s
p
-adic
L
-functions, and
IMC
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
follows again from Wiles’ theorem (see §
6.2
).

When
0<d^{+}<d
,
\rho
is non-critical and less is known. If
(d,d^{+})=(2,1)
and
\rho
is irreducible, then
\rho
comes from a classical weight one modular cuspform
f
. Under a regularity assumption on
f
, a potential candidate for
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
is proposed in Ferrara’s PhD thesis
[
Fer18
]
(for
f
dihedral) and in §
6.6
(for any
f
) where indirect evidence for
EX
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
and
IMC
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
are provided.

The subcase where
f
has complex multiplication by an imaginary quadratic field
k
, that is,
\rho
is induced from a character
\chi
of
k
, is better understood. Under mild assumptions on
\chi
, Conjecture
A
holds when
p
splits in
k
. The measure
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
is essentially a cyclotomic Katz
p
-adic
L
-function (§
6.4
-
6.5
) and
EZC
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
is an equivalent form of a
p
-adic Beilinson formula proven by Buyukboduk and Sakamoto. When
p
is inert in
k
,
EX
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
and
EZC
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
hold when
\chi
has prime-to-
p
order, yielding a new
p
-adic Beilinson formula in Corollary
A
.

The last setting we consider is when
(d,d^{+})=(3,1)
and
\rho=\ad(\rho_{g})
, where
\rho_{g}
comes from a weight one cuspform
g
. In §
6.7
, the terms
\Reg_{{\omega}^{+}_{p}}(\rho)
and
\mathcal{L}(\rho,\rho^{+})
are related in a precise manner to the Gross-Stark unit and the
p
-adic iterated integral attached to
g
in Darmon-Lauder-Rotger’s variant on the Gross-Stark conjecture
[
DLR16
]
. This suggests a plausible link between the conjectural element
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
and Hida’s adjoint
p
-adic
L
-function, and between
EZC
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
and Darmon-Lauder-Rotger’s conjecture.

We now outline the main results of this paper.

1.2.
The main results

We fix once and for all an odd prime
p
at which
\rho
is unramified. Our first result (Theorem
3.7.4
) generalizes
[
Mak21
, Théorème A]
and yields an interpolation formula for a generator of
\textrm{char}_{\Lambda}X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
which is similar to
EX
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
. As a piece of notation, we let
U_{\infty}=\varprojlim_{n}U_{n}
be the
\Lambda
-module of all norm-coherent sequences of elements in the pro-
p
completion
U_{n}
of the group of units of
H\cdot\mathbb{Q}_{n}
. It is a finitely generated
\Lambda
-module endowed with a linear action of
\Gal(H\cdot\mathbb{Q}_{\infty}/\mathbb{Q}_{\infty})
.

Theorem A
.

(1)

Fix a
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-stable
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-lattice
T_{p}
of
W_{p}
, a
p
-stabilization
\rho^{+}
of
\rho
and a
T_{p}
-optimal basis
\omega_{p}^{+}=t_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge t_{d^{+}}
of
\det W_{p}^{+}
. The following assertions are equivalent:

(i)

the Selmer group
X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
is of
\Lambda
-torsion,

(ii)

\rho^{+}
is
\eta
-admissible for some non-trivial character
\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}
, and

(iii)

\rho^{+}
is
\eta
-admissible for all but finitely many characters
\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}
.

Moreover, there always exists a
p
-stabilization
\rho^{+}
such that
X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
is of
\Lambda
-torsion.

(2)

If the equivalent conditions (i-ii-iii) hold and if
d^{+}>0
, then there exist
\Lambda
-linearly independent Galois-equivariant homomorphisms
\Psi_{1},\ldots,\Psi_{d^{+}}\colon T_{p}\longrightarrow U_{\infty}
which only depend on
T_{p}
, and there exists a generator
\theta^{\textrm{alg}}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
of
\textrm{char}_{\Lambda}X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
such that

\eta(\theta^{\textrm{alg}}_{\rho,\rho^{+}})=\frac{p^{(n-1)\cdot d^{+}}}{\det(\rho^{-})(\sigma_{p}^{n})}\cdot j\left(\frac{\tau(\eta)^{d^{-}}}{\tau(\rho\otimes\eta)}\right)\cdot\det\left(\log_{p}|\Psi_{j}(t_{i})|_{\eta}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq d^{+}}

for all non-trivial characters
\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}
of conductor
p^{n}
, where
|\cdot|_{\eta}:U_{\infty}\longrightarrow U_{n-1}
stands for a certain “
\eta
-projection” introduced in §
3.7
.

By the Baker-Brumer theorem, condition (iii) in Theorem
A
(1) holds if
\rho
is irreducible,
d^{+}=1
and
\rho^{+}
is motivic, so
X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
is of
\Lambda
-torsion. Theorem
A
(2) reduces the construction of
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
to the problem of finding “special elements”
\Psi_{1},\ldots,\Psi_{d^{+}}
related to Artin
L
-values. When
\rho
is monomial, these elements can be obtained from families of Rubin-Stark elements (see Theorem
C
below).

The proof of Theorem
A
makes essential use of the theory of Coleman maps
[
Col79
]
and of classical results on the structure of
U_{\infty}
.
We first compare
X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
with a Bloch-Kato-style Selmer group and we apply Poitou-Tate duality theorem. We then combine Hochschild-Serre’s exact sequence with classical results of Kuz’min
[
Kuz72
]
and Belliard
[
Bel02
]
on the Galois structure of global (
p
-)units, and also a construction of modified Coleman maps in order to recover some information on the structure of
X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
as a
\Lambda
-module.

The Tamagawa number conjecture of Bloch and Kato
[
BK90
]
expresses special values of motivic
L
-functions in terms of arithmetic invariants. The following result (Theorem
3.9.4
) is an instance of how one can tackle Bloch-Kato’s conjecture via Iwasawa theory.

Theorem B
.

Assume that there exist a
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-stable
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-lattice
T_{p}
of
W_{p}
and an admissible
p
-stabilization
\rho^{+}
of
\rho
such that
\mathcal{L}(\rho,\rho^{+})\neq 0
and for which Conjecture
A
holds. Then the
p
-part of the Tamagawa Number Conjecture (in the formulation of Fontaine and Perrin-Riou
[
FPR94
]
) for
\rho
is valid. In particular, for
p
not dividing the order of the image of
\rho
, one has

\dfrac{L^{*}\left(\rho^{*},0\right)}{\Reg_{\omega_{\infty}^{+}}(\rho)}\sim_{p}\#\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{p}[G_{\mathbb{Q}}]}(T_{p},\mathcal{O}_{p}\otimes\mathscr{C}\!\ell(H)),

where
a\sim_{p}b
means that
a
and
b
are equal up to a
p
-adic unit, and
\mathscr{C}\!\ell(H)
is the ideal class group of the field
H
cut out by
\rho
. Here, the bases
\omega_{\mathrm{f}}
and
\omega_{\infty}^{+}
used to compute
\Reg_{\omega_{\infty}^{+}}(\rho)
are chosen to be
T_{p}
-optimal.

The proof of Theorem
B
rests upon a comparison between
X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
and Benois’ definition of Perrin-Riou’s module of
p
-adic
L
-functions
[
Ben14a
]
. Once the relation between
X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
and Perrin-Riou’s theory is fully established, Theorem
B
follows from the techniques of
loc. cit.
involving a descent argument for Selmer complexes.

Our last result (Theorems
5.3.1
,
5.3.2
and
5.4.3
) compares Iwasawa-theoretic conjectures in
[
BKS17
, §3-4]
with Conjecture
A
in the monomial case.

Theorem C
.

Suppose that
\rho=\mathrm{Ind}_{k}^{\mathbb{Q}}\chi
, where
\chi:G_{k}\longrightarrow E^{\times}
is a non-trivial character of a number field
k
of prime-to-
p
order and conductor. Assume the Rubin-Stark conjecture over
{\mathbb{Q}}
and over
{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
for the family of characters
\chi\otimes\eta
,
{\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}}
.

(1)

For every
p
-stabilization
\rho^{+}
,
EX
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
holds true.

(2)

For every
p
-stabilization
\rho^{+}
such that
X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
is of
\Lambda
-torsion,
IMC
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
is equivalent to the higher-rank Iwasawa main conjecture (Conjecture
4.2.1
).

(3)

For every
p
-stabilization
\rho^{+}
,
EZC
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
is implied by the Iwasawa-theoretic Mazur-Rubin-Sano conjecture (Conjecture
4.2.3
).

(4)

Conversely, there exists a finite family
\rho^{+}_{1},\ldots,\rho^{+}_{t}
of
p
-stabilizations such that, if
EZC
{}_{\rho,\rho_{i}^{+}}
holds for all
1\leq i\leq t
, and if there exists at least one such
p
-stabilization
\rho_{j}^{+}
satisfying
\mathcal{L}(\rho,\rho_{j}^{+})\neq 0
, then the Iwasawa-theoretic Mazur-Rubin-Sano conjecture is valid.

See Theorem
5.4.3
(2) for a precise statement of the last assertion.

Let us comment on the statement and the proof of Theorem
C
. The
p
-adic
L
-function
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
should interpolate leading terms of abelian
L
-functions for which Rubin formulated a Stark conjecture “over
\mathbb{Z}
”
[
Rub96
]
. This involves some Rubin-Stark elements
(\varepsilon_{n}^{\chi})_{n\geq 1}
and
u^{\chi}
, and the (weaker) corresponding Rubin-Stark conjecture over
\mathbb{Q}
reduces to the validity of the statement (
2
) implicitly assumed in Conjecture
A
. Under the Rubin-Stark conjecture over
{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
, which is also assumed in the formulation of
[
BKS17
]
, the family
\varepsilon_{\infty}^{\chi}=\varprojlim_{n}\varepsilon_{n}^{\chi}
has the right
p
-integrality property and the first claim of Theorem
C
mainly follows from the machinery of refined Coleman maps. Our second claim is much in the spirit of theorems which compare a main conjecture “with
p
-adic
L
-functions” with a main conjecture “without
p
-adic
L
-functions”. Its proof makes use of what we call
extended Coleman maps
, together with a variant of the description of
X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
used for Theorems
A
and
B
. For the last two claims, we first need to compute the constant term of our extended Coleman maps. Roughly speaking, the Mazur-Rubin-Sano conjecture connects
u^{\chi}
and the bottom layer of
\varepsilon_{\infty}^{\chi}
, and can be seen as an equality
(\mathcal{E})
between elements in a certain finite-dimensional vector space
X
. We reinterpret
EZC
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
as the same equality, after applying a certain linear form on
X
attached to
\rho^{+}
. The third claim then becomes straightforward, and under the conditions of the last claim one can produce a generating set of linear forms
\nu
on
X
such that
\nu((\mathcal{E}))
holds. Moreover, the non-vanishing assumption on the
\mathcal{L}
-invariant ensures that the weak exceptional zero conjecture for
\chi
(Conjecture
4.2.2
) holds, which is a part of the Mazur-Rubin-Sano conjecture.

Theorem
C
provides strong evidence for Conjecture
A
, assuming the Rubin-Stark conjecture. As an application, we obtain the construction of plus-minus
p
-adic
L
-functions and a proof of a
p
-adic Beilinson formula in a new setting (see Corollary
6.5.2
for the general statement).

Corollary A
.

Let
k
be an imaginary quadratic field in which
p
is inert and let
\chi
be a character of
k
of prime-to-
p
order and conductor which does not descend to
\mathbb{Q}
. Assume
\chi(p\mathcal{O}_{k})=1
and, for simplicity, that
\sigma_{p}^{-1}\cdot\tau\in\ker\chi
, where
\tau\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
is the complex conjugation determined by the embedding
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\subset\mathbb{C}
. There exist two measures
\theta^{\pm}_{\chi}\in\Lambda
such that, for all characters
\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}
of conductor
p^{n}
, we have

(4)

\eta(\theta^{\pm}_{\chi})=\left(1\mp\frac{\eta(p)}{p}\right)\left(1\pm{\eta(p)}\right)\frac{(\pm 1)^{n}}{\tau(\eta)}\log_{p}((u_{\chi\eta})^{\pm 1}\cdot\bar{u}_{\chi\eta})\cdot j\left(\frac{L^{\prime}((\chi\eta)^{-1},0)}{\log_{\infty}(u_{\chi\eta}\cdot\bar{u}_{\chi\eta})}\right),

where
u_{\chi\eta}
is a certain
\chi\eta
-unit (
38
) and
\bar{u}_{\chi\eta}
denotes its complex conjugate.

Moreover,
\mathds{1}(\theta^{-}_{\chi})=0
, and we have

(5)

\dfrac{\diff}{\diff\!s}\kappa^{s}(\theta^{-}_{\chi})\bigg|_{s=0}=\left(1+\frac{1}{p}\right)\frac{2}{\mathrm{ord}_{p}(u_{\mathfrak{p},\chi})}\det\begin{pmatrix}\log_{p}(u_{\chi})&\log_{p}(u_{{\mathfrak{p}},\chi})\\
\log_{p}(\bar{u}_{\chi})&\log_{p}(\bar{u}_{{\mathfrak{p}},\chi})\end{pmatrix}\cdot j\left(\frac{L^{\prime}(\chi^{-1},0)}{\log_{\infty}(u_{\chi}\cdot\bar{u}_{\chi})}\right),

where
u_{{\mathfrak{p}},\chi}
is an analogue of a Gross-Stark unit (
39
).

The interpolation property (
4
) follows from Rubin-Stark’s conjecture for imaginary quadratic fields, while (
5
) is derived from the recent work of Bullach and Hofer
[
BH21
]
on the Iwasawa-theoretic Mazur-Rubin-Sano conjecture.

We conclude this introduction with the proof of the Gross-Kuz’min conjecture for abelian extensions of imaginary quadratic fields (Theorem
6.5.5
).

Theorem D
.

Let
p
be an odd prime and let
H
be an abelian extension of an imaginary quadratic field
k
. Denote by
H_{\infty}
the
{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
-cyclotomic extension of
H
, and by
M_{\infty}
the maximal abelian pro-
p
extension of
H_{\infty}
that is unramified everywhere and completely split at
p
. Then the module of
\Gal(H_{\infty}/H)
-coinvariants of
\Gal(M_{\infty}/H_{\infty})
is finite.

So far, the Gross-Kuz’min conjecture has only been settled for abelian extensions of
\mathbb{Q}

[
Gre73
]
and under some rather restrictive assumptions on the splitting behaviour of
p
and
\infty
in the number field
[
Jau02
,
Kuz18
,
Kle19
]
. Besides its applications to classical Iwasawa theory
[
Kuz72
,
Gro81
,
FG81
,
Kol91
,
FMD05
,
Jau17
]
, the Gross-Kuz’min conjecture is also an essential input in the strategy presented in
[
BKS17
]
for proving the equivariant Tamagawa Number Conjecture (eTNC).

By the descent formalism of Selmer complexes, the Gross-Kuz’min conjecture is implied by the non-vanishing of certain
\mathcal{L}
-invariants (Theorem
3.9.8
(2)). This implication can be seen as a generalization to arbitrary number fields of classical results by Kolster and Federer-Gross for totally real and CM fields. The proof then consists in showing that these
\mathcal{L}
-invariants do not vanish. To do so, we adapt a trick in
[
BD21
]
which ultimately relies on Roy’s strong six exponential theorem
[
Roy92
]
.

1.3.
Organisation of the paper

The plan of the paper is as follows. In §
2
we define extended Coleman maps and we compute their constant terms. In §
3
we introduce the objects which play a role in Conjecture
A
and we develop tools from Iwasawa theory in order to study our Selmer groups. In §
4
we recall some conjectures on Rubin-Stark elements that appear in the statement of Theorem
C
. We study in §
5
the precise link between these conjectures and Conjecture
A
. In §
6
we specialize our study to various classical contexts, and we also give an application of our results to the Gross-Kuz’min conjecture.

2.
Coleman Maps

2.1.
Classical results on Coleman maps

Fix an odd prime number
p
. Let
K
be an unramified finite extension of
{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
and let
\varphi\in\Gal(K/{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
be the Frobenius automorphism.
The Galois group
\Gal(K(\mu_{p^{\infty}})/{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
splits into a product
\Gal(K/{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})\times\Gamma_{{\cyc}}\simeq\Gal(K/{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})\times\Gamma\times\Gal(K(\mu_{p})/K)
, where we have put
\Gamma_{{\cyc}}=\Gal(K(\mu_{p^{\infty}})/K)
and where
\Gamma
is the Galois group of
{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
-cyclotomic extension of
K
.
The cyclotomic character induces an isomorphism
\chi_{\cyc}:\Gamma_{{\cyc}}\simeq\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}
and
\Gamma
can be identified with
1+p{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\subset\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}
. Let us fix once and for all a system of compatible roots of unity
\tilde{\zeta}:=(\zeta_{n})_{n\geq 0}
of
p
-power order (
i.e.
,
\zeta_{n+1}^{p}=\zeta_{n}
,
\zeta_{0}=1
and
\zeta_{1}\neq 1
). Note that
\tilde{\zeta}
is also norm-coherent, which means that it lives in the inverse limit
\varprojlim_{n}{K(\mu_{p^{n}})}^{\times}
where the transition maps are the local norms. Coleman proved the following theorem (see
[
Col79
, Thm. A]
or
[
dS87
, Chapter I, §2]
).

Theorem 2.1.1

(Coleman)
.

Let
v=(v_{n})_{n\geq 0}\in\varprojlim_{n}{K(\mu_{p^{n}})}^{\times}
. There exists a unique power series
f_{v}(T)\in T^{\mathrm{ord}(v_{0})}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{K}[[T]]^{\times}
, called
Coleman’s power series of
v
, which satisfies the following properties:

•

for all
n\geq 1
, one has
f_{v}(\zeta_{n}-1)=\varphi^{n}(v_{n})
,

•

\prod_{i=0}^{p-1}f_{v}(\zeta_{1}^{i}(1+T)-1)=\varphi(f_{v})((1+T)^{p}-1)
, where we let
\varphi
act on the coefficients of
f_{v}
.

Furthermore, the map
v\mapsto f_{v}(T)
is multiplicative and it is compatible with the action of
\Gamma_{{\cyc}}
, i.e., one has
f_{\gamma(v)}(T)=f_{v}((1+T)^{\chi_{\cyc}(\gamma)}-1)
for all
\gamma\in\Gamma_{\cyc}
.

Note that
f_{v}(0)
and
v_{0}
are not equal but are simply related by the formula
v_{0}=(1-\varphi^{-1})f_{v}(0)
which is easily deduced from the second property satisfied by
f_{u}
. To better understand Coleman’s power series, we consider the operator
\mathcal{L}:\mathcal{O}_{K}[[T]]^{\times}\longrightarrow K[[T]]
given by

\mathcal{L}(f)(T)=\frac{1}{p}\log_{p}\left(\frac{f(T)^{p}}{\varphi(f)((1+T)^{p}-1)}\right),

where the
p
-adic logarithm
\log_{p}:\mathcal{O}_{K}[[T]]^{\times}\longrightarrow K[[T]]
is defined by
\log_{p}(\zeta)=0
for
\zeta\in\mu(K)
and
\log_{p}(1+Tg(T))=Tg(T)-\frac{1}{2}T^{2}g(T)^{2}+\frac{1}{3}T^{3}g(T)^{3}+\ldots
for
g(T)\in\mathcal{O}_{K}[[T]]
. Moreover, it is known that
\mathcal{L}
takes values in
\mathcal{O}_{K}[[T]]
.

We can now define what is usually referred to as the Coleman map. One can associate to any
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-valued measure
\lambda
over
{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
its Amice transform
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}(T)\in\mathcal{O}_{K}[[T]]
given by

\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}(T)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\binom{x}{n}T^{n}\lambda(x)=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(1+T)^{x}\lambda(x).

This construction yields an isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebras (the product of measures being the convolution product)
\mathcal{O}_{K}[[{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}]]\simeq\mathcal{O}_{K}[[T]]
. When the Amice transform of a measure
\lambda
is equal to
\mathcal{L}(f_{u}(T))
for some norm-coherent sequence of
units

u\in\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{O}_{K(\mu_{p^{n}})}^{\times}
, then one can show that
\lambda
is actually the extension by zero of a measure over
\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}
. It is convenient to see such a
\lambda
as a measure over
\Gamma_{{\cyc}}
after taking a pull-back by
\chi_{\cyc}
. Let

\Coleman:\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{O}_{K(\mu_{p^{n}})}^{\times}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{K}[[\Gamma_{\cyc}]]

be the map sending
u
to the
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-valued measure
\lambda
over
\Gamma_{\cyc}
whose Amice transform is
\mathcal{L}(f_{u}(T))
. Note that
\Coleman(uu^{\prime})=\Coleman(u)+\Coleman(u^{\prime})
for norm-coherent sequences of units
u
and
u^{\prime}
.

Proposition 2.1.2
.

There is an exact sequence of
\Gal(K/{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})\times\Gamma_{\cyc}
-modules

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mu(K)\times{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{O}_{K(\mu_{p^{n}})}^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Coleman}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{K}[[\Gamma_{\cyc}]]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

Here, the first map sends
\xi\in\mu(K)
to
(\xi)_{n}
and
a\in{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
to
(\zeta_{n}^{a})_{n}
, and the last one sends
\lambda
to
\Tr_{K/{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\int_{\Gamma_{\cyc}}\chi_{\cyc}(\sigma)\lambda(\sigma)
. Moreover,
\Gamma_{{\cyc}}
acts on
\mathcal{O}_{K}[[\Gamma_{\cyc}]]
via
\int_{\Gamma_{\cyc}}f(\sigma)(\gamma\cdot\lambda)(\sigma)=\int_{\Gamma_{\cyc}}f(\gamma\sigma)\lambda(\sigma)
for all
\gamma\in\Gamma_{{\cyc}}
and all
{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-valued continuous functions
f
on
\Gamma_{\cyc}
.

Proof.

See
[
CS06
, Thm. 3.5.1]
.
∎

Now take invariants by
\Delta:=\Gal(K(\mu_{p})/K)
. As
\Delta
is of prime-to-
p
order, one can identify
\mathcal{O}_{K}[[\Gamma_{\cyc}]]^{\Delta}
with
\mathcal{O}_{K}[[\Gamma]]
via the map sending
\lambda
to the measure
\mu
defined by
\int_{\Gamma}g(\gamma)\mu(\gamma)=\frac{1}{p-1}\int_{\Gamma_{\cyc}}g(\sigma\!\!\!\mod\Delta)\lambda(\sigma)
for all continuous
g:\Gamma\longrightarrow{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. The inverse map is given by the formula
\int_{\Gamma_{\cyc}}f(\sigma)\lambda(\sigma)=\int_{\Gamma}Tf(\gamma)\mu(\gamma)
, where
f:\Gamma_{\cyc}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
is continuous and where
Tf:\Gamma\longrightarrow{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
is the sum
Tf(\gamma)=\sum_{\delta\in\Delta}f(\gamma\delta)
.
Let us denote by
K_{n}=K(\mu_{p^{n+1}})^{\Delta}
the
n
-th layer of the
{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
-cyclotomic extension of
K
for all
n\geq 0
, and let
K_{-1}=K_{0}=K
. The Coleman map thus restricts to a surjective map
\Coleman:\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{O}_{K_{n-1}}^{\times}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{K}[[\Gamma]]
whose kernel is identified with
\mu(K)
. By restricting further to principal units
\mathcal{O}^{\times,1}_{K_{n}}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{K_{n}}^{\times}
(
i.e.
, units that are congruent to 1 modulo the maximal ideal of
\mathcal{O}_{K_{n}}
), one obtains an isomorphism

(6)

\Coleman:\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{O}^{\times,1}_{K_{n-1}}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{O}_{K}[[\Gamma]]

of
{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}[\Gal(K/{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})][[\Gamma]]
-modules (or, equivalently, of
\varphi
-linear
{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}[[\Gamma]]
-modules) whose properties are now recalled. For
n\geq 2
, one may see via
\iota_{p}
any non-trivial
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
-valued Dirichlet character
\eta
of
p
-power order and of conductor
p^{n}
as a
p
-adic character of
\Gamma_{n-1}=\Gal(K_{n-1}/K)
which does not factor through
\Gamma_{n-2}
. We denote by
e_{\eta}:=p^{1-n}\sum_{g\in\Gamma_{n-1}}\eta(g^{-1})g\in{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\mu_{p^{n}})[\Gamma_{n-1}]
the idempotent attached to
\eta
, and by
\mathfrak{g}(\eta)=\sum_{a\mod p^{n}}\eta(a)\zeta_{n}^{a}
its usual Gauss sum.

Lemma 2.1.3
.

Let
u=(u_{n})_{n\geq 1}\in\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{O}^{\times,1}_{K_{n-1}}
and let
\mu=\Coleman(u)
. For all non-trivial characters
\eta:\Gamma\longrightarrow\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\times}
of conductor
p^{n}
, one has

\int_{\Gamma}\eta(\gamma)\mu(\gamma)=\frac{p^{n-1}}{\mathfrak{g}(\eta^{-1})}\cdot\varphi^{n}\left(e_{\eta}\log_{p}(u_{n})\right).

Moreover, if
u_{0}\neq 1
, then
u_{0}\notin 1+p{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
and one has

\int_{\Gamma}\mu=\frac{1-p^{-1}\varphi}{1-\varphi^{-1}}\left(\log_{p}(u_{1})\right).

Proof.

When
\eta
is non-trivial, one checks that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}(\eta^{-1})\ \int_{\Gamma}\eta(\gamma)\mu(\gamma)

\displaystyle=(p-1)^{-1}\ \sum_{a\mod p^{n}}\eta^{-1}(a)\cdot\mathcal{L}(f_{u})(\zeta_{n}^{a}-1)

\displaystyle=p^{n-1}\cdot e_{\eta}\ \mathcal{L}(f_{u})(\zeta_{n}-1)

\displaystyle=p^{n-1}\cdot e_{\eta}\ \varphi^{n}\left(\log_{p}(u_{n})-\frac{1}{p}\log_{p}(u_{n-1})\right).

Since
\eta
is of conductor
p^{n}
, the idempotent
e_{\eta}
kills
u_{n-1}
, so the first equality holds. Assume now that
u_{0}\neq 1
. If
u_{0}\in 1+p{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
, then
u_{0}^{[K:{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}]}=N_{K/{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}(u_{0})
would be a universal norm in
\mathbb{Q}_{p,\infty}/{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. As it is also a principal unit, the exact sequence (
7
) below shows that it should be equal to
1
, and so does
u_{0}
. Hence
u_{0}\notin 1+p{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
, and we have

\int_{\Gamma}\mu=(p-1)^{-1}\cdot\mathcal{L}(f_{u})(0)=(p-1)^{-1}\cdot(1-p^{-1}\varphi)\log_{p}(f_{u}(0))=\frac{1-p^{-1}\varphi}{1-\varphi^{-1}}\left(\log_{p}(u_{1})\right),

because of the relation
u_{1}^{p-1}=u_{0}=(1-\varphi^{-1})f_{u}(0)
.
∎

2.2.
Isotypic components

Let
\delta:G\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}^{\times}
be any character of a finite group
G
which takes values in a finite flat extension
\mathcal{O}
of
{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
. There are two slightly different notions of
\delta
-isotypic components for an
\mathcal{O}[G]
-module
M
, namely

M^{\delta}:=\{m\in M\ \big|\ \forall g\in G,\ g.m=\delta(g)m\},\quad\textrm{or,}\quad M_{\delta}:=M\otimes_{\mathcal{O}[G]}\mathcal{O},

where the ring homomorphism
\mathcal{O}[G]\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}
is the one induced by
\delta
. The modules
M^{\delta}
and
M_{\delta}
are respectively the largest submodule and the largest quotient of
M
on which
G
acts via
\delta
(see
[
Tsu99
, §2]
) and they are simply called the
\delta
-part and the
\delta
-quotient of
M
. When
p
does not divide the order of
G
, the natural map
M^{\delta}\longrightarrow M_{\delta}
is an isomorphism, and
M^{\delta}
equals
e_{\delta}M
, where
e_{\delta}=\#(G)^{-1}\sum_{g\in G}\delta(g^{-1})g\in\mathcal{O}[G]
is the usual idempotent attached to
\delta
.

When
\mathcal{O}
contains
\mathcal{O}_{K}
and for
G=\Gal(K/{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
,
M=\mathcal{O}_{K}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}
as in §
2.1
, the same argument as in the proof of
[
Mak21
, Lemme 3.2.3]
shows that the internal multiplication
M\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}
induces by restriction an
\mathcal{O}
-linear isomorphism
M^{\delta}\simeq\mathcal{O}
. When one moreover fixes an isomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{K}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}[G]
(given by a normal integral basis of the unramified extension
K/{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
) one also has
M_{\delta}\simeq\mathcal{O}[G]\otimes_{\mathcal{O}[G]}\mathcal{O}=\mathcal{O}
.

Definition 2.2.1
.

For any character
\delta
of
G=\Gal(K/{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
with values in a finite flat
{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
-algebra
\mathcal{O}
containing
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, we define
\delta
-isotypic component of the Coleman map to be the composite isomorphisms of
\mathcal{O}[[\Gamma]]
-modules

\Coleman^{\delta}:\varprojlim_{n}\left(\mathcal{O}^{\times,1}_{K_{n-1}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}\right)^{\delta}\overset{\Coleman}{\longrightarrow}\left(\mathcal{O}_{K}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}\right)^{\delta}[[\Gamma]]\simeq\mathcal{O}[[\Gamma]],

where
\Coleman
is the restriction of isomorphism (
6
) to the
\delta
-parts and the last isomorphism is induced by the internal multiplication
\mathcal{O}_{K}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}
.

When
\delta
is trivial, it will later be convenient to consider a natural extension of the map
\Coleman^{\mathds{1}}
which we construct in the rest of the paragraph.

We let
\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{p,n}
be the pro-
p
completion of
\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{p,n}
for all
n\geq 0
. Concretely, once we fix a uniformizer
\varpi_{n}
of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p,n}:={\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p,n}}
, we have
\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{p,n}={\mathbb{Z}}^{\times,1}_{p,n}\times\varpi_{n}^{{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}
, while
\mathbb{Q}_{p,n}^{\times}={\mathbb{Z}}^{\times}_{p,n}\times\varpi_{n}^{\mathbb{Z}}
. Let
\mathcal{A}:=\ker\left({\mathbb{Z}_{p}}[[\Gamma]]\longrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\right)
be the augmentation ideal of the Iwasawa algebra
{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}[[\Gamma]]
. We still denote by
\gamma
the image of an element
\gamma\in\Gamma
under the canonical injection
\Gamma\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}[[\Gamma]]^{\times}
.

Lemma 2.2.2
.

The multiplication map

m:\mathcal{A}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}[[\Gamma]]}\varprojlim_{n}\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{p,n-1}\otimes\mathcal{O}\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{n}\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{p,n-1}\otimes\mathcal{O}

given by
a\otimes v\otimes 1\mapsto a\cdot v\otimes 1
is injective and has image
\varprojlim_{n}\mathbb{Z}^{\times,1}_{p,n-1}\otimes\mathcal{O}
.

Proof.

We may assume
\mathcal{O}={\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
. We first check that
m
is injective and we fix a topological generator
\gamma
of
\Gamma
. As
\mathcal{A}
is
{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}[[\Gamma]]
-free and generated by
\gamma-1
, any element of
\mathcal{A}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}[[\Gamma]]}\varprojlim_{n}\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{p,n-1}
can be written as a pure tensor
(\gamma-1)\otimes v
for some
v
. If
(\gamma-1)\cdot v=1
, then
v_{n}\in\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{p}
for all
n\geq 1
hence
v=1
since
\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{p}
has no non-trivial
p
-divisible element. Thus
m
is injective.
Inflation and restriction maps in discrete group cohomology provide an exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Gamma,{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{1}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}},{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p,\infty},{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})^{\Gamma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{2}(\Gamma,{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\inf}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{res}}

As
\Gamma
is pro-cyclic, the last term vanishes. Moreover, the Galois action on
{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
being trivial, all the
\mathrm{H}^{1}
’s involved are
\Hom
’s and one easily deduces from local class field and from exactness of Pontryagin functor
\Hom(-,{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})
(see e.g.
[
Flo79
, Thm. 6]
) the following short exact sequence

(7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\varprojlim_{n}\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{p,n-1}\right)_{\Gamma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v\mapsto v_{1}\;\;}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rec\;\;}

where the subscript
\Gamma
means that we took
\Gamma
-coinvariants and where
\rec
is the local reciprocity map. Since
M_{\Gamma}=M/\mathcal{A}M
for any
{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}[[\Gamma]]
-module
M
and since the map
\rec_{|1+p{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}
is an isomorphism, it follows that both
\varprojlim_{n}\mathbb{Z}^{\times,1}_{p,n-1}
and the image of
m
coincide with the kernel of the map
\varprojlim_{n}\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{p,n-1}\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{p}
given by
v\mapsto v_{1}
, so they are equal.
∎

Let
\mathcal{O}
be any finite flat
{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
-algebra and let
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}}\subseteq\Frac(\mathcal{O}[[\Gamma]])
be the principal ideal of
\mathcal{O}[[\Gamma]]
consisting of quotients of
p
-adic measures on
\Gamma
with at most one simple pole at the trivial character. Then
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}}
is invertible, and its inverse is the augmentation ideal
\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{O}}=\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{O}
of
\mathcal{O}[[\Gamma]]
. The following definition makes sense by Lemma
2.2.2
.

Definition 2.2.3
.

The extended
\mathds{1}
-isotypic component of the Coleman map is the isomorphism

\widetilde{\Coleman}^{\mathds{1}}:\varprojlim_{n}\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{p,n-1}\otimes\mathcal{O}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{O}}

given by
\widetilde{\Coleman}^{\mathds{1}}(v)=\frac{1}{a}\Coleman^{\mathds{1}}(av)
for any non-zero element
a\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{O}}
. As
\Coleman^{\mathds{1}}
is
\mathcal{O}[[\Gamma]]
-linear, this definition does not depend on the choice of
a
.

For any non-trivial
\mathcal{O}
-valued character
\delta
of
\Gal(K/{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
, we also let
\widetilde{\Coleman}^{\delta}=\Coleman^{\delta}
.

2.3.
Constant term of Coleman maps

We keep the same notations as in §
2.1
and §
2.2
. Fix a finite flat extension
\mathcal{O}
of
{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
which contains
\mathcal{O}_{K}
and let
\delta
be an
\mathcal{O}
-valued character of
\Gal(K/{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
. Then
\delta
is the trivial character if and only if
\beta=\delta(\varphi)\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}
is equal to
1
. When
\delta
is non-trivial, Lemma
2.1.3
shows that the constant term of
\mu=\Coleman^{\delta}(u)
is given by

\int_{\Gamma}\mu=\frac{1-p^{-1}\beta}{1-\beta^{-1}}\log_{p}(u_{1}).

We now give a similar formula when
\delta
is trivial which is a variant of
[
Ben14b
, Prop. 1.3.7]
.

Lemma 2.3.1
.

Let
u\in\varprojlim_{n}\mathbb{Z}^{\times,1}_{p,n-1}\otimes\mathcal{O}
and put
\mu=\Coleman^{\mathds{1}}(u)
. Write
u
as
(\gamma-1)\cdot v
for some
\gamma\in\Gamma
and
v\in\varprojlim_{n}\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{p,n-1}\otimes\mathcal{O}
. Then

(8)

\int_{\Gamma}\mu=\left(1-p^{-1}\right)\cdot\log_{p}(\chi_{\cyc}(\gamma))\cdot\mathrm{ord}_{p}(v_{1}),

where
\mathrm{ord}_{p}:\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{p}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}
is the usual
p
-valuation map.

Proof.

We may take
\mathcal{O}={\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
. Let us choose any
\pi=(\pi_{n})_{n\geq 1}\in\varprojlim_{n}\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{p,n-1}
such that
\pi_{1}=p
(this is possible thanks to the short exact sequence (
7
)). Then
\pi\in\varprojlim_{n}\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{p,n-1}
and
f_{\pi}(T)=T^{p-1}U(T)
for some
U(T)\in{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}[[T]]^{\times}
, so we have

f_{(\gamma-1)\cdot\pi}(T)=\left(\frac{(1+T)^{\chi_{cyc}(\gamma)}-1}{T}\right)^{p-1}\cdot\frac{U((1+T)^{\chi_{cyc}(\gamma)}-1)}{U(T)}.

Hence
\log_{p}(f_{(\gamma-1)\cdot\pi}(0))=(p-1)\cdot\log_{p}(\chi_{\cyc}(\gamma))
. If we write
v
as
u^{\prime}\cdot\pi^{\mathrm{ord}_{p}(v_{1})}
with
u^{\prime}\in\varprojlim_{n}\mathbb{Z}^{\times,1}_{p,n-1}
, then one has by linearity

\begin{split}\int_{\Gamma}\mu&=\int_{\Gamma}\Coleman^{\mathds{1}}((\gamma-1)\cdot u^{\prime})\quad\;\qquad+\mathrm{ord}_{p}(v_{1})\int_{\Gamma}\Coleman^{\mathds{1}}((\gamma-1)\cdot\pi)\\
&=\int_{\Gamma}\left(\gamma\cdot\Coleman^{\mathds{1}}(u^{\prime})-\Coleman^{\mathds{1}}(u^{\prime})\right)+\mathrm{ord}_{p}(v_{1})(p-1)^{-1}\cdot\mathcal{L}(f_{(\gamma-1)\cdot\pi})(0)\\
&=0\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\!\!+\mathrm{ord}_{p}(v_{1})(1-p^{-1})(p-1)^{-1}\cdot\log_{p}(f_{(\gamma-1)\cdot\pi}(0))\\
&=\left(1-p^{-1}\right)\cdot\log_{p}(\chi_{\cyc}(\gamma))\cdot\mathrm{ord}_{p}(v_{1}).\end{split}

∎

Remark 2.3.2
.

The dependence on
\gamma
in (
8
) is only apparent. Indeed, assume

(\gamma-1)\cdot v=(\gamma^{c}-1)\cdot v^{\prime}

for
\gamma\in\Gamma-\{1\}
,
c\in{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}-\{0\}
and
v,v^{\prime}\in\varprojlim_{n}\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}_{p,n-1}^{\times}
. Then
\log_{p}(\chi_{\cyc}(\gamma^{c}))=c\cdot\log_{p}(\chi_{\cyc}(\gamma))
, and

\mathrm{ord}_{p}(v_{1}^{\prime})=\mathrm{ord}_{p}((\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma^{c}-1}\cdot v)_{1})=\mathrm{ord}_{p}(((c^{-1}+(*)(\gamma-1))\cdot v)_{1})=\mathrm{ord}_{p}(v_{1}^{c^{-1}})=c^{-1}\cdot\mathrm{ord}_{p}(v_{1}),

because
v_{1}^{\gamma-1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,1}
. Thus we see that the
c
’s cancel out, as predicted by (
8
).

3.
Cyclotomic Iwasawa theory for Artin motives

Thoughout this section we keep the notations of the introduction: in particular,
\rho
does not contain the trivial representation and is unramified at our fixed prime
p>2
. Without loss of generality, we also assume that
E
contains the field
H
cut out by
\rho
, so the completion
E_{p}=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\iota_{p}(E)\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
contains
K:=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\iota_{p}(H)\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
.

3.1.
p
-stabilizations

Definition 3.1.1
.

A
p
-stabilization

(\rho^{+},W_{p}^{+})
of
(\rho,W_{p})
is an
E_{p}
-linear subspace
W_{p}^{+}
of
W_{p}
of dimension
d^{+}
which is stable under the action of the local Galois group
\Gal(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}/{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
. We say that
\rho^{+}
is:

(1)

motivic
if
W_{p}^{+}
is of the form
E_{p}\otimes_{E,\iota_{p}}W^{+}
, where
W^{+}
is an
E
-linear subspace of
W
, and

(2)

\eta
-
admissible
(for a given character
\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}
) if the
p
-adic pairing (
3
) is non-degenerate.

To explain in greater details the
\eta
-admissibility property, consider a
p
-stabilization
W_{p}^{+}
and fix a character
\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}
. If we let
\omega_{\mathrm{f},\eta}=\psi_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\psi_{d^{+}}
be a basis of
\det_{E}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}((\rho\otimes\eta)^{*}(1))=\det_{E}\Hom_{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}(W_{\eta},E_{\eta}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{H_{\eta}}^{\times})
and
\omega_{p}^{+}=w_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge w_{d^{+}}
be a basis of
\det_{E_{p}}W_{p}^{+}
, then the determinant of the
p
-adic pairing computed in these bases is given by

(9)

\Reg_{\omega_{p}^{+}}(\rho\otimes\eta)=\det\left(\log_{p}(\psi_{j}(w_{i}))\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq d^{+}}\in E_{p,\eta}.

Here, we denoted by
E_{p,\eta}\subseteq\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}
the compositum of
E_{p}
with the image of
\eta
, and by

(10)

\log_{p}:\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Z}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\times}\longrightarrow\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}

the map given by
\log_{p}(x\otimes a)=x\cdot\log_{p}^{\textrm{Iw}}(\iota_{p}(a))
, where
\log_{p}^{\textrm{Iw}}:\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\times}_{p}\longrightarrow\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}
is Iwasawa’s
p
-adic logarithm. The
p
-regulator
\Reg_{\omega_{p}^{+}}(\rho\otimes\eta)
does not vanish for (some, hence) all choices of bases if and only
W_{p}^{+}
is
\eta
-admissible.

We now recall a classical conjecture in
p
-adic transcendence theory
[
CM09
, Conjecture 3.10]
:

Conjecture 3.1.2

(Weak
p
-adic Schanuel conjecture)
.

Let
a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}
be
n
non-zero algebraic numbers contained in a finite extension
F
of
{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. If
\log_{p}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(a_{1}),\ldots,\log_{p}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(a_{n})
are linearly independent over
\mathbb{Q}
, then the extension field
\mathbb{Q}(\log_{p}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(a_{1}),\ldots,\log_{p}^{\mathrm{Iw}}(a_{n}))\subset F
has transcendence degree
n
over
\mathbb{Q}
.

Lemma 3.1.3
.

Assume that
\rho
is irreducible and that
W_{p}^{+}
is motivic. Then
W_{p}^{+}
is
\eta
-admissible for all characters
\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}
if
d^{+}=1
, or if Conjecture
3.1.2
holds.

Proof.

Since
W_{p}^{+}
is motivic, one may choose a basis
\omega_{p}^{+}=w_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge w_{d^{+}}
of
\det_{E_{p}}W_{p}^{+}
with
w_{i}\in W
for
1\leq i\leq d^{+}
. Fix a character
\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}
and let
e_{\eta}
be the idempotent associated with the irreducible representation
\rho\otimes\eta
. We have an isomorphism of
E_{\eta}[G_{\mathbb{Q}}]
-modules

f\colon e_{\eta}\cdot\left(E_{\eta}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{H_{\eta}}^{\times}\right)\simeq(W_{\eta})^{\oplus d^{+}},

which we fix. We then define a basis
\omega_{\mathrm{f},\eta}=\psi_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\psi_{{d^{+}}}
of
\det_{E_{\eta}}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}((\rho\otimes\eta)^{*}(1))
by letting
\psi_{i}=f^{-1}\circ p_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq d^{+}
, where
p_{i}\colon W_{\eta}\hookrightarrow W_{\eta}^{d^{+}}
is the inclusion of the
i
-th copy of
W_{\eta}
inside
W_{\eta}^{d^{+}}
. Observe that
\psi_{i}
is injective by construction and that the linear subspaces
\im\psi_{1},\ldots,\im\psi_{d^{+}}
are in direct sum, so the family
(\psi_{j}(w_{i}))_{i,j}
is
E_{\eta}
-linearly independent. Brumer’s theorem
[
Bru67
]
implies that the restriction of (
10
) to
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\otimes\mathcal{O}_{H_{\eta}}^{\times}
is injective, so (
9
) expresses
\Reg_{\omega_{p}^{+}}(\rho\otimes\eta)
as a polynomial in
p
-adic logarithms of units in
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\otimes\mathcal{O}_{H_{\eta}}^{\times}
that are
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
-linearly independent. Therefore,
\Reg_{\omega_{p}^{+}}(\rho\otimes\eta)
does not vanish if Conjecture
3.1.2
holds, or if
d^{+}=1
.
∎

We end this section by exploring the link between the
\eta
-admissibility of a
p
-stabilization
W_{p}^{+}
and the Leopoldt conjecture for
H_{\eta}
and
p
. As in
[
DM22
, Prop. 2.9(ii)]
,
W_{p}^{+}
is
\eta
-admissible if and only if
E_{p,\eta}\otimes W_{p}^{+}
does not meet the linear subspace

W_{p}^{\circ}=\bigcap_{\psi}\ker\left[\log_{p}\circ\psi:W_{p,\eta}\longrightarrow E_{p,\eta}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{H_{\eta}}^{\times}\longrightarrow E_{p,\eta}\right],

where the intersection runs over all
\psi
in
E_{p,\eta}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}((\rho\otimes\eta)^{*}(1))
. The dimension of
W_{p}^{\circ}
can be written as
d^{-}+s
, where
s
is the dimension of the kernel of the map

\alpha_{\eta}:E_{p,\eta}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}((\rho\otimes\eta)^{*}(1))\longrightarrow\Hom(W_{p,\eta},E_{p,\eta})

induced by
\log_{p}
.
Kummer theory (as in the proof of Lemma
3.6.2
) provides a natural isomorphism

(11)

\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q},W_{p,\eta}^{*}(1))\simeq\Hom_{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}(W_{p,\eta},E_{p,\eta}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{H_{\eta}}^{\times})=E_{p,\eta}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}((\rho\otimes\eta)^{*}(1))

between the domain of
\alpha_{\eta}
and the usual Bloch-Kato Selmer group of the arithmetic dual
W_{p,\eta}^{*}(1)
of
W_{p,\eta}
. One may also see
W_{p,\eta}^{*}(1)
as
G_{{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
-representation and consider the local Bloch-Kato Selmer group
\mathrm{H}^{1}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}},W_{p,\eta}^{*}(1))
, which is, again by Kummer theory, canonically isomorphic to
\Hom_{G_{{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}}(W_{p,\eta},E_{p,\eta}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\times,1}_{K_{\eta}})
, where
\mathcal{O}^{\times,1}_{K_{\eta}}
is the
{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
-module of principal units of the completion
K_{\eta}
at
p
of
H_{\eta}
. Under the above identifications, the map
\alpha_{\eta}
is nothing but the composite map

(12)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q},W_{p,\eta}^{*}(1))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},W_{p,\eta}^{*}(1))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom(W_{p,\eta},E_{p,\eta}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{loc}_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where
\mathrm{loc}_{p}
is the localization map at
p
, and the second map the isomorphism induced by the
p
-adic logarithm on
K_{\eta}
,
\log_{p}:E_{p,\eta}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\times,1}_{K_{\eta}}\longrightarrow E_{p,\eta}
. Let
\mathcal{U}_{p,\eta}
be the product of the principal units
\mathcal{O}^{\times,1}_{K^{\prime}_{\eta}}
of
K^{\prime}_{\eta}
, where
K^{\prime}_{\eta}
runs over all the completions of
H_{\eta}
at primes above
p
. There is an alternative description of
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},W_{p,\eta}^{*}(1))
in terms of semi-local Galois cohomology which identifies
\mathrm{loc}_{p}
of (
12
) with the map

(13)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}(W_{p,\eta},E_{p,\eta}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{H_{\eta}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}(W_{p,\eta},E_{p,\eta}\otimes\mathcal{U}_{p,\eta})}

induced by the diagonal embedding
\iota_{\textrm{Leo}}:\mathcal{O}_{H_{\eta}}^{\times}\longrightarrow\mathcal{U}_{p,\eta}
. The injectivity of
\iota_{\textrm{Leo}}
, known as the Leopoldt conjecture, implies the injectivity of the map in (
13
) which should be thought as the “
\rho\otimes\eta
-isotypic component of the Leopoldt conjecture for
H_{\eta}
and
p
”.

Lemma 3.1.4
.

W_{p}
admits at least one
\eta
-admissible
p
-stabilization if and only if the
\rho\otimes\eta
-isotypic component of the Leopoldt conjecture for
H_{\eta}
and
p
holds, i.e., if the map in (
13
) is injective.

Proof.

There exists at least one
\eta
-admissible
p
-stabilization of
W_{p}
if and only if the linear subspace
W_{p}^{\circ}
has dimension
d^{-}
, that is, if the map
\alpha_{\eta}
is injective. But we have seen that its injectivity is equivalent to the one of the map in (
13
).
∎

3.2.
Selmer groups

A Galois-stable lattice of
W_{p}
is a free
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-submodule of
W_{p}
of rank
d
which is stable by the action of the global Galois group
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
. The pair
(\rho,\rho^{+})
will always refer to the choice of:

(1)

a Galois-stable lattice
T_{p}
of
W_{p}
,

(2)

a
p
-stabilization
W_{p}^{+}
of
\rho
,

which we will be fixed henceforth. Let
W_{p}^{-}=W_{p}/W_{p}^{+}
,
T_{p}^{+}=W_{p}^{+}\bigcap T_{p}
and
T_{p}^{-}=T_{p}/T_{p}^{+}
. We also define
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-divisible Galois modules
D_{p}=W_{p}/T_{p}
and
D_{p}^{\pm}=W_{p}^{\pm}/T_{p}^{\pm}
. Once we fix a generator of the different of
\mathcal{O}_{p}
over
{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
, one may identify
\mathcal{O}_{p}^{\vee}
with
E_{p}/\mathcal{O}_{p}
where
M^{\vee}:=\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(M,{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})
stands for the Pontryagin dual of a
{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
-module
M
. This allows us to identify
D_{p}^{\vee}
with
T_{p}
. Let
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
and let
I_{\ell}
be the inertia subgroup of
\Gal(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu/\mathbb{Q}_{n})
at the place above
\ell
determined by
\iota_{\ell}
.

Definition 3.2.1
.

The Selmer group of level
n
attached to
(\rho,\rho^{+})
is defined to be

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\Sel_{n}(\rho,\rho^{+})&:=&\ker\left[\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{n},D_{p})\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}(I_{p},D_{p}^{-})\times\prod_{\ell\neq p}\mathrm{H}^{1}(I_{\ell},D_{p})\right].\end{array}

The strict Selmer group
\Sel_{n}^{\mathrm{str}}(\rho,\rho^{+})
of level
n
attached to
(\rho,\rho^{+})
is the sub-
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-module of
\Sel_{n}(\rho,\rho^{+})
whose cohomology classes are trivial on the decomposition subgroup at
p
.

The dual Selmer group is defined as the Pontryagin dual of
\Sel_{n}(\rho,\rho^{+})
, that is,

X_{n}(\rho,\rho^{+}):=\Sel_{n}(\rho,\rho^{+})^{\vee}=\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Sel_{n}(\rho,\rho^{+}),{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}).

We also define the strict dual Selmer group of level
n
by putting
X_{n}^{\mathrm{str}}(\rho,\rho^{+})=\Sel_{n}^{\mathrm{str}}(\rho,\rho^{+})^{\vee}
.

By standard properties of discrete cohomology groups,
X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
can be identified with the inverse limit
\varprojlim_{n}X_{n}(\rho,\rho^{+})
and it is a finitely generated module over the Iwasawa algebra
\Lambda=\mathcal{O}_{p}[[\Gamma]]
.

Lemma 3.2.2
.

If
X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
is of
\Lambda
-torsion, then there is a short exact sequence of torsion
\Lambda
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{0}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}},T_{p}^{-})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{str}}(\rho,\rho^{+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0,}

where the
\Gamma
-action on the first term is trivial.

Proof.

The first map is obtained by evaluating cocycles at
\sigma_{p}
and by applying Pontryagin duality, and the second one is the dual of the inclusion
\Sel_{\infty}^{\mathrm{str}}(\rho,\rho^{+})\subseteq\Sel_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
. The only non-obvious statement is the injectivity of the first map, which will follow from
[
GV00
, Prop. (2.1)]
, but we must check that
\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{\infty},\widecheck{D}_{p})
is finite, where
\widecheck{D}_{p}=\Hom(T_{p},\mu_{p^{\infty}})
. Since
\rho
is unramified at
p
and since it does not contain the trivial representation,
\mathrm{H}^{0}({\mathbb{Q}(\mu_{p^{\infty}})},D_{p})
is finite, so
\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{\infty},\widecheck{D}_{p})\subseteq\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{Q}(\mu_{p^{\infty}}),\widecheck{D}_{p})=\Hom(\mathrm{H}^{0}({\mathbb{Q}(\mu_{p^{\infty}})},D_{p}),\mu_{p^{\infty}})
is also finite, as wanted.
∎

The study of the structure of
X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
was investigated in
[
Mak21
]
, where the unramifiedness assumption was partially released (only the quotient
W_{p}^{-}
was assumed to be unramified). However,
\rho
was taken irreducible and
\rho^{+}
motivic. These last hypotheses imply the
\eta
-admissibility of
\rho^{+}
for all characters
\eta
under the Weak
p
-adic Schanuel conjecture (or when
d^{+}=1
) as in Lemma
3.1.3
. We recall the results obtained in
loc. cit.
.

Theorem 3.2.3
.

Assume that
\rho
is irreducible and that
\rho^{+}
is motivic. If
d^{+}=1
or if Conjecture
3.1.2
holds, then the following four claims are true.

(1)

The Selmer groups
X_{n}(\rho,\rho^{+})
are finite for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

(2)

The
\Lambda
-module
X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
is torsion and has no non-trivial finite submodules.

(3)

Let
\theta^{\textrm{alg}}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}\in\Lambda
be a generator of its characteristic ideal. Then
\eta(\theta^{\textrm{alg}}_{\rho,\rho^{+}})
does not vanish for all non-trivial finite order characters
\eta:\Gamma\longrightarrow\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\times}
.

(4)

Let
e=\dim\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},W_{p}^{-})
. Then
\theta^{\textrm{alg}}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
vanishes at the trivial character
\mathds{1}
if and only if
e=0
. Moreover,
\theta^{\textrm{alg}}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
has a zero of order
\geq e
at
\mathds{1}
, i.e.,

\theta^{\textrm{alg}}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}\in\mathcal{A}^{e},

where
\mathcal{A}
is the augmentation ideal of
\Lambda
.

Proof.

This follows from
[
Mak21
, Théorème 2.1.5]
.
∎

3.3.
Artin
L
-functions and Galois-Gauss sums

We review some classical results on Artin
L
-functions and on Galois-Gauss sums, and we give equivalent reformulations of Conjecture
A
. Our main reference is
[
Mar77
]
. Let
(V,\pi)
be an Artin representation of
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
of dimension
d
and of Artin conductor
\mathrm{f}(\pi)
. Put
d^{+}=\dim\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{R},V)
and
d^{-}=d-d^{+}
. The Artin
L
-function of
\pi
is the meromorphic continuation to
s\in\mathbb{C}
of the infinite product (converging for
\Re(s)>1
) over all rational primes

L(\pi,s)=\prod_{\ell}\det(1-\ell^{-s}\sigma_{\ell}\ \big|\ V^{I_{\ell}})^{-1},

where
\sigma_{\ell}
is the Frobenius substitution at
\ell
and
I_{\ell}\subseteq G_{\mathbb{Q}}
is any inertia group at
\ell
. It is known to satisfy a functional equation which can compactly be written
\Lambda(\pi,1-s)=W(\pi)\Lambda(\pi^{*},s)
, where
W(\pi)
is Artin’s root number and
\Lambda(\pi,s)
is the “enlarged”
L
-function. By definition,
\Lambda(\pi,s)
is equal to the product
\mathrm{f}(\pi)^{s/2}\Gamma(\pi,s)L(\pi,s)
, where
\Gamma(\pi,s)=\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s)^{d^{+}}\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s+1)^{d^{-}}
(and
\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s):=\pi^{-s/2}\Gamma(s/2)
) is the
L
-factor at
\infty
of
\pi
. The Galois-Gauss sum of
\pi
is defined as

\tau(\pi)=i^{d^{-}}\sqrt{\mathrm{f}(\pi)}W(\pi^{*})=i^{d^{-}}\dfrac{\sqrt{\mathrm{f}(\pi)}}{W(\pi)}

(see
[
Mar77
, Chapter II, Definition 7.2]
and the remark that follows). In particular, when
\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{Q},V)=0
, the functional equation yields

(14)

L^{*}(\pi^{*},0):=\lim_{s\rightarrow 0}L(\pi^{*},s)/s^{d^{+}}=\frac{\tau(\pi)}{2^{d^{+}}}\dfrac{L(\pi,1)}{(-i\pi)^{d^{-}}}.

Lemma 3.3.1
.

We keep the notations of Conjecture
A
. The interpolation property of
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
can be written as follows:

\textbf{EX}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
:

for all non-trivial characters
\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}
of exact conductor
p^{n}
, one has

\displaystyle\eta(\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}})

\displaystyle=\det(\rho^{-})(\sigma_{p}^{-n})\ {\Reg_{\omega_{p}^{+}}(\rho\otimes\eta)}\cdot j\left(\frac{\tau(\eta)^{d^{-}}}{2^{d^{+}}}\ \dfrac{L\left(\rho\otimes\eta,1\right)}{(-i\pi)^{d^{-}}\Reg_{\omega_{\infty}^{+}}(\rho\otimes\eta)}\right).

\textbf{EZC}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
:

If
W_{p}^{+}
is admissible, then
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
has a trivial zero at the trivial character
\mathds{1}
of order at least
e
, and one has

\displaystyle\frac{1}{e!}\dfrac{\diff^{e}}{\diff\!s^{e}}\kappa^{s}(\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}})\bigg|_{s=0}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{e}\ \mathcal{L}(\rho,\rho^{+})\ \mathcal{E}(\rho,\rho^{+})\ \Reg_{\omega_{p}^{+}}(\rho)\ j\left(\dfrac{L\left(\rho,1\right)}{2^{d^{+}}(-i\pi)^{d^{-}}\Reg_{\omega_{\infty}^{+}}(\rho)}\right).

Proof.

This follows from (
14
) applied to
\pi=\rho\otimes\eta
.
∎

Lemma 3.3.2
.

(1)

If
\chi
is a Dirichlet character, then
\tau(\chi)
is the usual Gauss sum of
\chi^{-1}
, i.e.,
\tau(\chi)=\mathfrak{g}(\chi^{-1})
.

(2)

We have
\tau(\pi)\in F^{\times}
for any splitting field
F\subseteq\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
of
\pi
.

(3)

If
\pi
is unramified at
p
, then
\tau(\pi)
is a
p
-adic unit.

(4)

Take
\pi=\rho\otimes\eta
with
\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}
and put
N=\mathrm{f}(\rho)
,
p^{n}=\mathrm{f}(\eta)
. Then

\tau(\rho\otimes\eta)=\tau(\rho)\cdot\mathfrak{g}(\eta^{-1})^{d}\cdot\det(\rho)^{-1}(\sigma_{p}^{n})\cdot\eta^{-1}(N).

Proof.

The first statement follows from the well-known fact that
\mathfrak{g}(\chi^{-1})=i^{d^{-}}W(\chi^{-1})\mathrm{f}(\chi)^{1/2}
, and the second statement from Fröhlich’s theorem
[
Mar77
, Chapter II, Theorem 7.2]
. For the third statement, recall first that
\tau(\pi)
is a product over all primes
\ell
of local Galois-Gauss sums
\tau(\pi_{\ell})\in\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\times}
attached to the local representation
\pi_{\ell}
over
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
associated with
\pi
(see
loc.cit.
, Chapter II, Proposition 7.1). We claim that
\tau(\pi_{\ell})
is only divisible by primes above
\ell
and that
\tau(\pi_{p})=1
. Local Galois-Gauss sums are defined with the aid of Brauer induction from the case of multiplicative characters
\theta
of
\Gal(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\ell}/M)
for a finite extension
M/\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
(see
loc.cit.
, Chapter II, §4. and §2.). It is known that
\tau(\theta)
is an algebraic integer dividing the norm of the local conductor (which is a power of
\ell
), and moreover that
\tau(\theta)=1
whenever both
\theta
and
M/\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
are unramified. This implies easily our claim and (3) as well. Since
\rho
and
\eta
have coprime conductors, the statement (4) follows from
loc. cit.
, Chapter IV, Exercise 3b).
∎

Proposition 3.3.3
.

(1)

The statement
EX
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
in Conjecture
A
is equivalent to the existence of an element
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}^{\prime}\in\Frac(\Lambda)
which has at most a pole at
\mathds{1}
and which satisfies the following interpolation property: for all non-trivial characters
\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}
of conductor
p^{n}
, we have

\eta(\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}^{\prime})=\frac{\det(\rho^{+})(\sigma_{p}^{n})}{\mathfrak{g}(\eta^{-1})^{d^{+}}}\ \Reg_{\omega_{p}^{+}}(\rho\otimes\eta)\cdot j\left(\dfrac{L^{*}\left((\rho\otimes\eta)^{*},0\right)}{\Reg_{\omega_{\infty}^{+}}(\rho\otimes\eta)}\right).

Moreover, if
EX
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
holds, then
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
and
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}^{\prime}
are equal up to multiplication by a unit of
\Lambda
.

(2)

If
EX
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
holds and if
W_{p}^{+}
is admissible, then
EZC
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
is equivalent to

\frac{1}{e!}\dfrac{\diff^{e}}{\diff\!s^{e}}\kappa^{s}(\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}^{\prime})\bigg|_{s=0}=(-1)^{e}\ \mathcal{E}(\rho,\rho^{+})\ \mathcal{L}(\rho,\rho^{+})\ \Reg_{\omega_{p}^{+}}(\rho)\cdot j\left(\dfrac{L^{*}\left(\rho^{*},0\right)}{\Reg_{\omega_{\infty}^{+}}(\rho)}\right).

Proof.

By Lemma
3.3.2
(4), the two quotients of
p
-adic measures
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
and
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}^{\prime}
are related (when they exist) by the formula
\theta_{N}\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}^{\prime}=\tau(\rho)\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
, where we have written
\theta_{N}=\prod_{\ell|N}\gamma_{\ell}^{\mathrm{ord}_{\ell}(N)}\in\Gamma\subseteq\Lambda^{\times}
(and where
\gamma_{\ell}\in\Gamma
is equal to the restriction to
\mathbb{Q}_{\infty}
of
\sigma_{\ell}
). By Lemma
3.3.2
(3), one has
\tau(\rho)\in\mathcal{O}_{p}^{\times}
, so
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
and
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}^{\prime}
are equal up to a unit of
\Lambda
, and the proposition follows easily.
∎

3.4.
Stark’s regulator

Let
(\pi,V)
be a linear representation of
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
factoring through the Galois group of a finite extension
L/\mathbb{Q}
and with coefficients in a number field
F\subset\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\subset\mathbb{C}
. We denote by
S_{\infty}(L)
the set of archimedean places of
L
.

Given any finite set of primes
S
of
L
containing
S_{\infty}(L)
, we let
Y_{L,S}
be the free abelian group on the set
S
, and we define the subgroup

(15)

X_{L,S}:=\left\{\sum_{w}a_{w}\cdot w\in Y_{L,S}\ \biggm|\ \sum_{w}a_{w}=0\right\}.

Let
\mathcal{O}_{L,S}
be the ring of
S
-integers of
{L}
. By Dirichlet’s unit theorem, the regulator map

(16)

\lambda_{L,S}:\mathbb{R}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{L,S}^{\times}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{R}\otimes X_{L,S},\qquad a\mapsto-\sum_{w|v\in S}\log|a|_{w}w,

is a
\Aut(L)
-equivariant isomorphism (see
[
Rub96
, §1.1]
).

We now specialize to
S=S_{\infty}(L)
,
\mathcal{O}_{L,S}^{\times}=\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times}
and we put
X=X_{L,S_{\infty}(L)}
and
\lambda=\lambda_{L,S_{\infty}(L)}
.
By the Noether-Deuring theorem, there exists an isomorphism of
\mathbb{Q}[G_{\mathbb{Q}}]
-modules (which we fix)

(17)

f\ \colon\mathbb{Q}\otimes X\simeq\mathbb{Q}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{L}.

The determinant
R(\pi,f)\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
of the map

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\Hom_{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathbb{C}\otimes_{F,\iota_{\infty}}V,\mathbb{C}\otimes X)&\longrightarrow&\Hom_{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathbb{C}\otimes_{F,\iota_{\infty}}V,\mathbb{C}\otimes X)\\
\psi&\mapsto&\lambda\circ f\circ\psi\end{array}

is Stark’s regulator attached to
\pi^{*}
and
f
defined in
[
Tat84
, Chap. I, §4]
. Note that both
L(\pi^{*},s)
and
R(\pi,f)
implicitly depend on the embedding
\iota_{\infty}\colon\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
. Given any automorphism
\alpha
of
\mathbb{C}
, the map
\alpha\circ\iota_{\infty}
also defines a complex embedding, and we denote by
L((\pi^{\alpha})^{*},s)
and
R(\pi^{\alpha},f)
respectively the resulting
L
-series and Stark regulator.

Conjecture 3.4.1

(Stark’s conjecture)
.

For any automorphism
\alpha
of
\mathbb{C}
we have
A(\pi^{\alpha},f)=A(\pi,f)^{\alpha}
, where

A(\pi^{\alpha},f)=\frac{L^{*}((\pi^{\alpha})^{*},0)}{R(\pi^{\alpha},f)}.

In particular,
A(\pi,f)\in F^{\times}
by basic field theory. One can check that Conjecture
3.4.1
does not depend on the choice of the isomorphism
f
in (
17
).

Lemma 3.4.2
.

Assume that
\pi
does not contain the trivial representation. The determinant of the pairing

B\colon\left\{\begin{array}[]{rclcc}\mathbb{C}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{R},V)&\times&\mathbb{C}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\pi^{*}(1))&\longrightarrow&\mathbb{C}\\
(1\otimes v&,&1\otimes\psi)&\mapsto&\log_{\infty}(\psi(v)),\end{array}\right.

computed with respect to
\omega_{\infty}^{+}\in\det_{F}\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{R},V)
and
\omega_{\mathrm{f}}\in\det_{F}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\pi^{*}(1))
equals
R(\pi,f)\mod F^{\times}
.

Proof.

Recall that
X
sits inside
Y=\bigoplus_{w\in S_{\infty}(L)}\mathbb{Z}w
and note that
Y/X\simeq\mathbb{Z}
, the
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-action on
\mathbb{Z}
being trivial. Our hypothesis on
\pi
ensures that the natural embedding
\Hom_{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}(V,F\otimes X)\hookrightarrow\Hom_{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}(V,F\otimes Y)
is an isomorphism. Letting
w_{0}
be the archimedean place of
L
corresponding to
\iota_{\infty}
, we have
Y\simeq\mathrm{Ind}_{\Gal(\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R})}^{\Gal(L/\mathbb{Q})}\ \mathbb{Z}w_{0}
as a Galois module. By Shapiro’s lemma, we have

\Hom_{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}(V,F\otimes X)=\Hom_{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}(V,F\otimes Y)\simeq\Hom_{\Gal(\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R})}(V,F)=\Hom_{F}(\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{R},V),F),

where the isomorphism in the middle is induced by the projection map
p\colon Y\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
given by
p(\sum_{w}a_{w}w)=a_{w_{0}}
. Since
p\circ\lambda=-\log_{\infty}
on
\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{L}
, one sees that the determinant of
B
coincides with that of the map
\psi\mapsto p\circ\lambda\circ\psi
with respect to
\omega_{\mathrm{f}}
and
(\omega_{\infty}^{+})^{-1}\in\det_{F}\Hom_{F}(\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{R},V),F)
. Therefore, this determinant equals
\pm R(\pi,f)
if one chooses
f,\omega_{\mathrm{f}},\omega_{\infty}^{+}
so that
f^{*}(\omega_{\mathrm{f}})=p_{*}((\omega_{\infty}^{+})^{-1})
.
∎

Proposition 3.4.3
.

Assume Conjecture
3.4.1
. The validity of Conjecture
A
for
(\rho,\rho^{+})
is independent of the choice of the bases
\omega_{\infty}^{+}
,
\omega_{p}^{+}
and
\omega_{\mathrm{f},\eta}
(for
{\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}}
). More precisely, the measure
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}\in\mathcal{O}_{p}[[\Gamma]]
is well-defined up to multiplication by an element of
\mathcal{O}_{p}^{\times}
, and does not depend of the choice of the family
(\omega_{\mathrm{f},\eta})_{\eta}
.

Proof.

Note that the Galois-Gauss sums and the terms
\det(\rho^{-})(\sigma_{p}^{-n})
,
\mathcal{E}(\rho,\rho^{+})
and
\mathcal{L}(\rho,\rho^{+})
appearing in
EX
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
and
EZC
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
do not depend on any basis. We are reduced to study the dependence on the choice of bases of the quantity

(18)

\frac{\Reg_{\omega_{p}^{+}}(\rho\otimes\eta)}{j\left(\Reg_{\omega_{\infty}^{+}}(\rho\otimes\eta)\right)}\in\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}

for
\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}
. The quotient (
18
) equals the determinant with respect to
\omega_{\infty}^{+}
and
\omega_{p}^{+}
of the composition of maps

\mathbb{C}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{R},W)\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{1}((\rho\otimes\eta)^{*}(1))^{*}\simeq\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{1}((\rho\otimes\eta)^{*}(1))^{*}\longrightarrow\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}\otimes W_{p}^{+}

induced by the pairings (
1
), (
3
) and
j^{-1}\colon\mathbb{C}\simeq\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}
. In particular, (
18
) does not depend on the choice of
\omega_{\mathrm{f},\eta}
, and it is multiplied by
ab^{-1}
if one replaces
{\omega}_{\infty}^{+}
and
{\omega}_{p}^{+}
by
a\omega_{\infty}^{+}
and
b\omega_{p}^{+}
respectively (with
a,b\in E_{p}
). Therefore, the same conclusion holds for
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
, and the proposition follows from the fact that
a
and
b
belong to
\mathcal{O}_{p}^{\times}
by hypothesis of
T_{p}
-optimality.
∎

3.5.
Local and global duality

In order to better describe our Selmer group we first need to introduce and compare the “unramified condition” and the “
\mathrm{f}
-condition” of Bloch and Kato for (the dual of) a local Galois representation with finite image.

Take any
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-representation
T
of the absolute Galois group
G_{F}
