of a finite extension
F
of
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
(including
\ell=p
). Assume that
T
is of finite image,
i.e.
, the action of
G_{F}
factors through the Galois group
\Delta
of a finite extension
L/F
. Define
\textbf{D}=\textbf{T}\otimes{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
and
\widecheck{\textbf{T}}=\textbf{T}^{*}(1)
the arithmetic dual of
T
. Recall that, if
I\subseteq G_{F}
is the inertia subgroup of
G_{F}
and
M
is a
G_{F}
-module, then
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{ur}}(F,M)
is the kernel of the restriction map
\mathrm{H}^{1}(F,M)\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}(I,M)
.

Lemma 3.5.1
.

(1)

If
\ell\neq p
, then we have
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{ur}}(F,\widecheck{\textbf{T}})\subseteq\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(F,\widecheck{\textbf{T}})
and
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(F,{\textbf{D}})\subseteq\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{ur}}(F,{\textbf{D}})
.

(2)

If
\ell=p
and if
T
is unramified, then
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{ur}}(F,\textbf{D})=\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(F,{\textbf{D}})
.

(3)

Under the local Tate pairing
\mathrm{H}^{1}(F,\widecheck{\textbf{T}})\times\mathrm{H}^{1}(F,\textbf{D})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{p}\otimes{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
, the
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}
’s are orthogonal complements for any prime
\ell
and the
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{ur}}
’s are orthogonal complements for any
\ell\neq p
.

Proof.

The first point follows from
[
Rub00
, Lem. 3.5]
. Let us prove the second statement and assume now that
\ell=p
and that
T
is unramified. Recall that
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(F,\textbf{D})
is by definition the image of
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(F,\textbf{W})
under the map
\mathrm{H}^{1}(F,\textbf{W})\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}(F,\textbf{D})
, where we have put
\textbf{W}=\textbf{T}\otimes{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. Since
W
is unramified, it is easy to see that
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(F,\textbf{W})=\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{ur}}(F,\textbf{W})
. Moreover, the map
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{ur}}(F,\textbf{W})\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{ur}}(F,\textbf{D})
is surjective because it coincides with the projection map
\textbf{W}_{\Delta}\twoheadrightarrow\textbf{D}_{\Delta}
by
[
Rub00
, Lem. 3.2.(i)]
, where
(-)_{\Delta}
means that we took the
\Delta
-coinvariants. Therefore, we have
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{ur}}(F,\textbf{D})=\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(F,{\textbf{D}})
.

The third statement is standard (see
[
BK90
, Prop. 3.8]
for Bloch-Kato’s condition and
[
Rub00
, Prop. 4.3.(i)]
for the unramified one).
∎

For
F=\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
(for any prime
\ell
) and for any compact
\mathcal{O}_{p}[[G_{F}]]
-module
T
, define the Iwasawa cohomology along the
{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
-cyclotomic extension
F_{\infty}=\cup_{n}F_{n}
of
F
by letting

\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},*}(F,\textbf{T})&:=\varprojlim_{n}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{*}(F_{n},\textbf{T})\qquad\qquad(*\in\{\emptyset,\mathrm{f},\mathrm{ur}\}),\\
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f},p}(\mathbb{Q},\textbf{T})&:=\varprojlim_{n}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f},p}(\mathbb{Q}_{n},\textbf{T}),\end{array}

where the subscript
\mathrm{f},p
in the last global cohomology groups means that we relaxed the condition of being crystalline at
p
. We also use the standard notation
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},/\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},\textbf{T})
for the quotient
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},\textbf{T})/\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},\textbf{T})
. All these cohomology groups are finitely generated modules over
\Lambda
by Shapiro’s lemma.

We keep the notations of §
3.2
and we fix a Galois-stable lattice
T_{p}
of
W_{p}
and any
p
-stabilization
W_{p}^{+}
of
W_{p}
. By Maschke’s theorem we may identify
W_{p}^{-}
with a
\Gal(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}/{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
-stable complement of
W_{p}^{+}
in
W_{p}
. Let
\widecheck{T}_{p}=T_{p}(1)^{*}
(resp.
\widecheck{T}_{p}^{\pm}=T_{p}^{\pm}(1)^{*}
) be the arithmetic dual of
T_{p}
(resp. of
T_{p}^{\pm}
). In particular,
\widecheck{T}_{p}^{\pm}
is a free
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-submodule of
\widecheck{T}_{p}
of rank
d^{\pm}
. We will relate
\Sel^{\mathrm{str}}_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
and
\Sel_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
to the following three localization maps

\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathrm{Loc}_{+}^{\mathrm{str}}:&\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f},p}(\mathbb{Q},\widecheck{T}_{p})\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\widecheck{T}^{+}_{p})\\
\mathrm{Loc}_{+}:&\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q},\widecheck{T}_{p})\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f}}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}},\widecheck{T}_{p}^{+}),\\
\mathrm{Loc}_{+}^{\prime}:&\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f},p}(\mathbb{Q},\widecheck{T}_{p})\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}},\widecheck{T}_{p}^{+})\bigoplus\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},/\mathrm{f}}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}},\widecheck{T}_{p}^{-}).\end{array}

For
\ell\neq p
, it is known that the quotient of the absolute Galois group of
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell,\infty}
by its inertia subgroup
I_{\ell}
is of order prime to
p
, so the restriction map
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell,\infty},D)\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}(I_{\ell},D)
is injective. This, together with Lemma
3.5.1
, implies that

(19)

\varinjlim_{n}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell,n},D_{p})=\varinjlim_{n}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{ur}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell,n},D_{p})=0,\quad\textrm{and}\quad\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},\widecheck{T}_{p})=\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{ur}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},\widecheck{T}_{p})=\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},\widecheck{T}_{p}).

Hence, the Selmer group of
(\rho,\rho^{+})
fits into an exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sha_{\infty}^{1}(D_{p})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sel_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varinjlim_{n}\left(\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p,n},D_{p}^{+})\bigoplus\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p,n},D_{p}^{-})\right),}

where
\Sha_{\infty}^{1}(D_{p})=\ker\left[\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\infty},D_{p})\longrightarrow\prod_{\ell}\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell,\infty},D_{p})\right]
is the first Tate-Shafarevitch group. It follows from Poitou-Tate duality
[
Rub00
, Cor. 7.5]
that there is a commutative diagram of
\Lambda
-modules with short exact rows

(20)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sha_{\infty}^{1}(D_{p})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sel_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coker\left(\mathrm{Loc}_{+}^{\prime}\right)^{\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{ur}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\infty},D_{p})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sel_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coker\left(\mathrm{Loc}_{+}\right)^{\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sha_{\infty}^{1}(D_{p})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sel^{\mathrm{str}}_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coker\left(\mathrm{Loc}_{+}^{\mathrm{str}}\right)^{\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0,}

where
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{ur}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\infty},D_{p})=\ker\left[\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\infty},D_{p})\longrightarrow\prod_{\ell}\mathrm{H}^{1}(I_{\ell},D_{p})\right]
(and where
I_{\ell}
is the inertia subgroup of
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell,\infty}
).

We close this section by proving the Weak Leopoldt conjecture for both
W_{p}
and
\widecheck{W}_{p}=W_{p}^{*}(1)
. The proof is self-contained and it won’t use the running assumption that
\rho
is unramified at
p
(but
p
is still be assumed to be odd). Let
\Sigma
be a finite set of places of
\mathbb{Q}
containing
p
and all the primes at which
\rho
is ramified. For
i\in\mathbb{N}
and
\textbf{T}=T_{p}
or
\widecheck{T}_{p}
define the cohomology groups
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{Iw},\Sigma}(\mathbb{Q},\textbf{T})=\varprojlim_{n}\mathrm{H}^{i}(\mathbb{Q}_{\Sigma}/\mathbb{Q}_{n},\textbf{T})
, where
\mathbb{Q}_{\Sigma}/\mathbb{Q}
is the maximal extension of
\mathbb{Q}
which is unramified outside
\Sigma
and
\infty
. We also consider the second (compact) Tate-Shafarevich groups
\Sha_{\infty}^{2}(\textbf{T})=\ker\left[\mathrm{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q},\textbf{T})\longrightarrow\prod_{\ell}\mathrm{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},\textbf{T})\right]
.

Proposition 3.5.2
.

(1)

We have
\Sha_{\infty}^{2}(\widecheck{T}_{p})\simeq\Sha_{\infty}^{1}(D_{p})^{\vee}
as
\Lambda
-modules.

(2)

The Weak Leopoldt conjecture along
\mathbb{Q}_{\infty}/\mathbb{Q}
for
W_{p}
and
\widecheck{W}_{p}
holds, that is, the
\Lambda
-modules
\mathrm{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw},\Sigma}(\mathbb{Q},T_{p})
and
\mathrm{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw},\Sigma}(\mathbb{Q},\widecheck{T}_{p})
are torsion.

Proof.

The first statement follows from Poitou-Tate duality (see, for instance,
[
Mil86
, Thm. 4.10.(a)]
). Let us first prove the Weak Leopoldt conjecture for
W_{p}
. By
[
PR95
, Prop. 1.3.2]
, it is equivalent to
\mathrm{H}^{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{\Sigma}/\mathbb{Q}_{\infty},D_{p})=0
. As
\Gal(\mathbb{Q}_{\Sigma}/\mathbb{Q}_{\infty})
has cohomological dimension 2, this module is
p
-divisible because
D_{p}
is. It is therefore enough to show that
\mathrm{H}^{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{\Sigma}/\mathbb{Q}_{\infty},D_{p})^{\vee}
is a torsion
\Lambda
-module, which has already been shown in the proof of
[
Mak21
, Prop. 2.5.5]
. Finally, since the Weak Leopoldt Conjecture is clearly invariant by Tate twists, its validity for
\widecheck{W}_{p}
follows from the one for the contragredient representation
W_{p}^{*}
.
∎

3.6.
Limits of unit groups

The various cohomology groups introduced in §
3.5
can usefully be described in terms of ideal class groups and of unit groups.

Notation 3.6.1
.

Let
n\geq 0
be an integer and let
w
be a
p
-adic place of
H
. We still denote by
w
the unique place of
H_{n}
above
H
, and we let

•

A_{n}
be the
p
-part of the ideal class group of
{H_{n}}
, and
A^{\prime}_{n}
its quotient by all the classes of
p
-adic primes of
{H_{n}}
,

•

\mathcal{O}_{H_{n}}^{\times}
(resp.
\mathcal{O}_{H_{n}}[\tfrac{1}{p}]^{\times}
) be the unit group (resp. the group of
p
-units) of
{H_{n}}
,

•

U_{n}
(resp.
U^{\prime}_{n}
) be the pro-
p
completion of the unit group (resp. of the group of
p
-units) of
{H_{n}}
,

•

U_{n,w}
(resp.
U^{\prime}_{n,w}
) be the pro-
p
completion of the unit group (resp. of the group of non-zero elements) of
{H_{n,w}}
.

We also let
A_{\infty},A^{\prime}_{\infty},U_{\infty},U^{\prime}_{\infty},U_{\infty,w},U^{\prime}_{\infty,w}
respectively be the projective limits of the preceding groups, where the transition maps are the (global or local) norm maps. All of these groups are
{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
-modules but we keep the same notations for the
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-modules that are obtained after tensoring with
\mathcal{O}_{p}
by a slight abuse of notation. They are all endowed with a natural action of the Galois group
\Gal(H_{\infty}/\mathbb{Q})\simeq G\otimes\Gamma
, so they have a structure of
\Lambda[G]
-modules.

Fix as in §
3.2
a Galois-stable
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-lattice
T_{p}
and a
p
-stabilization
W_{p}^{+}
of
W_{p}
. We also keep the notations of §
3.5
.

Lemma 3.6.2
.

Let
G_{p}
be the decomposition subgroup of
G
at the place
w
determined by
\iota_{p}
and let
\bullet\in\{\emptyset,+,-\}
.

(1)

The restriction maps on cohomology groups induce the following natural isomorphisms:

\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f},p}(\mathbb{Q},\widecheck{T}_{p})\simeq\Hom_{G}(T_{p},U^{\prime}_{\infty}),\qquad\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\widecheck{T}^{\bullet}_{p})\simeq\Hom_{G_{p}}(T^{\bullet}_{p},U^{\prime}_{\infty,w}),

\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q},\widecheck{T}_{p})\simeq\Hom_{G}(T_{p},U_{\infty}),\qquad\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\widecheck{T}^{\bullet}_{p})\simeq\Hom_{G_{p}}(T^{\bullet}_{p},U_{\infty,w}).

(2)

The
\Lambda
-modules
\Sha_{\infty}^{1}(D_{p})^{\vee}
and
\Hom_{G}(T_{p},A^{\prime}_{\infty})
are isomorphic after tensoring with
{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
(as
\Lambda\otimes{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-modules). They are isomorphic as
\Lambda
-modules if we assume that
p
does not divide the order of
G
.

(3)

The
\Lambda
-modules
\Sha_{\infty}^{1}(D_{p})
and
\Hom_{G}(A_{\infty}^{\prime},D_{p})
are pseudo-isomorphic.

Proof.

We will derive the isomorphisms from Kummer theory and from the injectivity (resp. bijectivity) of restriction maps between certain local (resp. global) cohomology groups. Fix an integer
n\geq 0
, a prime number
\ell
(including
\ell=p
) and let
\lambda|\ell
be the prime of
H_{n}
determined by
\iota_{\ell}
. As in §
3.5
, let
\mathbb{Q}_{\Sigma}
be the largest extension of
\mathbb{Q}
(or, equivalently, the largest extension of
H_{n}
) which is unramified outside
\Sigma
and
\infty
. Consider the finite field extension
F^{\prime}/F
and the Galois groups
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{G}
given either by

•

H_{n}/\mathbb{Q}_{n}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}=\Gal(\mathbb{Q}_{\Sigma}/H_{n})
,
\mathcal{G}=\Gal(\mathbb{Q}_{\Sigma}/\mathbb{Q}_{n})\qquad
(first case),

•

or by
H_{n,w}^{\mathrm{ur}}/\mathbb{Q}_{n,w}^{\mathrm{ur}}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}=\Gal(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}/H_{n,w}^{\mathrm{ur}})
,
\mathcal{G}=\Gal(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{n,w}^{\mathrm{ur}})\qquad
(second case),

•

or by
H_{n,\lambda}/\mathbb{Q}_{n,\lambda}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}=\Gal(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\ell}/H_{n,\lambda})
,
\mathcal{G}=\Gal(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\ell}/\mathbb{Q}_{n,\lambda})\qquad
(third case).

Note that
\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
can be identified with
G
in the first case (because
p
is unramified in
H/\mathbb{Q}
) and with
G_{p}
in the third case when
\ell=p
. Since
F^{\prime}
never contains
\mathbb{Q}(\mu_{p^{\infty}})
, we have in all three cases
\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\widecheck{T}_{p})=T_{p}^{*}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(1))=0
. Moreover, Hochschild-Serre’s spectral sequence applies to
\widecheck{T}_{p}
in the first case and the third case with
\ell\neq p
by
[
Rub00
, Appendix B, Proposition 2.7]
, so the restriction map

\mathrm{H}^{i}(\mathcal{G},\widecheck{T}_{p})\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathrm{H}^{i}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\widecheck{T}_{p})),\qquad(i=1,2)

is bijective when
i=1
, and surjective when
i=2
. It is also injective when
i=1
in all three cases by the inflation-restriction exact sequence.

Let us first look at the first isomorphism of (1). By taking inverse limits in the first case with
i=1
, the restriction map gives an isomorphism
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\Sigma}(\mathbb{Q},\widecheck{T}_{p})\simeq\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\Sigma}(H,\widecheck{T}_{p})^{G}
. Moreover, the local computation in (
19
) implies that
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\Sigma}(\mathbb{Q},\widecheck{T}_{p})=\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f},p}(\mathbb{Q},\widecheck{T}_{p})
and
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\Sigma}(H,\widecheck{T}_{p})=\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f},p}(H,\widecheck{T}_{p})
. We are reduced to study the module
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f},p}(H,\widecheck{T}_{p})^{G}
which we compute as follows. Since
G_{H}
acts trivially on
T_{p}
, it is equal to
\Hom_{G}(T_{p},\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f},p}(H,\mathcal{O}_{p}(1)))
. But Kummer theory naturally identifies
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f},p}(H,\mathcal{O}_{p}(1))
with
U^{\prime}_{\infty}
, so our claim follows. The three other restriction maps in (1) are shown to be isomorphisms by invoking similar arguments.

We now study
\Sha_{\infty}^{1}(D_{p})^{\vee}
, which is known to be isomorphic to
\Sha_{\infty}^{2}(\widecheck{T}_{p})
by Proposition
3.5.2
. The Hochschild-Serre spectral sequence provides in our setting a commutative diagram with exact rows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{1}(G,\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\Sigma}(H,\widecheck{T}_{p}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw},\Sigma}(\mathbb{Q},\widecheck{T}_{p})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw},\Sigma}(H,\widecheck{T}_{p})^{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{\ell\in\Sigma}\mathrm{H}^{1}(G,\prod_{\lambda|\ell}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(H_{\lambda},\widecheck{T}_{p}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{\ell\in\Sigma}\mathrm{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},\widecheck{T}_{p})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\prod_{\lambda|\ell\in\Sigma}\mathrm{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(H_{\lambda},\widecheck{T}_{p})\right)^{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}

The cohomology groups
\mathrm{H}^{1}(G,-)
on the left are killed by the order
\#G
of
G
, so they vanish after tensoring with
{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
or whenever
p
is coprime to
\#G
. Therefore, in order to prove the claim (2) it suffices to check that the module
\Sha_{\infty}^{2}(H,\widecheck{T}_{p})^{G}=\ker\alpha
can be identified with
\Hom_{G}(T_{p},A_{\infty}^{\prime})
. But again,
G_{H}
acts trivially on
T_{p}
so it is enough to see that
\Sha_{\infty}^{2}(H,\mathcal{O}_{p}(1))\simeq A_{\infty}^{\prime}
, which is classical (see for instance
[
Nek06
, (9.2.2.2)]
).

The proof of last statement also uses inflation-restriction and is nearly identical to the proof of
[
Mak21
, Lemme 2.2.2]
, once we identify
A_{\infty}^{\prime}
with the Galois group of the maximal abelian pro-
p
extension of
H_{\infty}
which is unramified everywhere and in which all primes above
p
split completely.
∎

Lemma 3.6.3
.

Assume that
U
is a free
\Lambda
-module of finite rank endowed with a
\Lambda
-linear action of
G
. Then the module
Z=\Hom_{G}(T_{p},U)
is also
\Lambda
-free.

Proof.

First recall that any finitely generated
\Lambda
-module
V
is free if and only if
V^{\Gamma}
vanishes and
V_{\Gamma}
is
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-free (see for example
[
Bel02
, Lemme 1.1]
). We already have
Z^{\Gamma}=\Hom_{G}(T_{p},U^{\Gamma})=0
. It is therefore enough to check that
Z_{\Gamma}
has no
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-torsion, and this will follow from the fact that
Z_{\Gamma}
injects into the torsion-free
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-module
\Hom_{G}(T_{p},U_{\Gamma})
. Let us first check that
(\gamma-1)\Hom_{G}(T_{p},U)=\Hom_{G}(T_{p},(\gamma-1)U)
, where
\gamma
is a topological generator of
\Gamma
. The inclusion
\subseteq
is obvious, so we only consider the reverse inclusion
\supseteq
and we take
\alpha\in\Hom_{G}(T_{p},(\gamma-1)U)
. As
T_{p}
is
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-free, one may write
\alpha=(\gamma-1)\beta
for some
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-linear map
\beta:T_{p}\longrightarrow U
. As the
G
-action is assumed to be
\Lambda
-linear, for all
g\in G
and
t\in T_{p}
we have
\beta(g.t)-g.\beta(t)\in U^{\Gamma}=0
, so
\beta\in\Hom_{G}(T_{p},U)
and
\alpha\in(\gamma-1)\Hom_{G}(T_{p},U)
as claimed. Therefore, we have

Z_{\Gamma}=\Hom_{G}(T_{p},U)/(\gamma-1)\Hom_{G}(T_{p},U)=\Hom_{G}(T_{p},U)/\Hom_{G}(T_{p},(\gamma-1)U)\\
\hookrightarrow\Hom_{G}(T_{p},U/(\gamma-1)U)=\Hom_{G}(T_{p},U_{\Gamma}),

as wanted.
∎

Lemma 3.6.4
.

(1)

The
\Lambda
-modules
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f},p}(\mathbb{Q},\widecheck{T}_{p})
and
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q},\widecheck{T}_{p})
are both free of rank
d^{+}
.

(2)

If
\coker\left(\mathrm{Loc}_{+}\right)
is of
\Lambda
-torsion, then the modules
\coker\left(\mathrm{Loc}_{+}^{\prime}\right)
,
\coker\left(\mathrm{Loc}_{+}^{\prime}\right)
and the Selmer groups
X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
and
X^{\mathrm{str}}_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
are all of
\Lambda
-torsion, and moreover
\ker\left(\mathrm{Loc}_{+}\right)=\ker\left(\mathrm{Loc}_{+}^{\prime}\right)=\ker\left(\mathrm{Loc}_{+}^{\mathrm{str}}\right)=0
.

Proof.

Since
H
is unramified at
p
, we have
\mu_{p}\not\subseteq H
so the
\Lambda
-modules
U_{\infty}
and
U^{\prime}_{\infty}
are free by
[
Bel02
, Théorème 1.5 and Corollaire 1.6]
. Thus, both Iwasawa cohomology groups are
\Lambda
-free by Lemmas
3.6.2
and
3.6.3
. Moreover, the validity of the Weak Leopoldt conjecture (Proposition
3.5.2
(2)) implies that they are both of rank
d^{+}
over
\Lambda
. Let us treat (2) and assume that
\coker\left(\mathrm{Loc}_{+}\right)
is of
\Lambda
-torsion. Note that the
\Lambda
-module
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{ur}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\infty},D_{p})
is co-torsion because
A_{\infty}
is of
\Lambda
-torsion by Iwasawa’s classical result
[
Iwa73
]
. Thus, the claims of torsionness of (2) all follow from the commutative diagram (
20
). Moreover, the source and the target of
\mathrm{Loc}_{+}
have the same rank and the source is torsion-free, so
\ker\left(\mathrm{Loc}_{+}\right)
must vanish. Since the two other kernels are submodules of
\ker\left(\mathrm{Loc}_{+}\right)
, they must be trivial as well.
∎

3.7.
Torsionness of Selmer groups

For any character
\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}
factoring through
{\Gamma}_{m}
but not through
\Gamma_{m-1}
for some
m\geq 0
, let
M[\eta]=M\otimes_{\Lambda,\eta}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}
be the
\eta
-isotypic component of a
\Lambda
-module
M
, and let

|\cdot|_{\eta}:\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}U^{\prime}_{\infty}&\longrightarrow&U^{\prime}_{m}[\eta]\\
(u_{n})_{n\geq 0}&\mapsto&e_{\eta}.u_{m},\end{array}\right.

where
e_{\eta}\in\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}[\Gamma_{m}]
is the idempotent attached to
\eta
(see §
2.1
), and where
U_{m}
is seen as a
\Lambda
-module via the projection map
\Lambda\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{p}[\Gamma_{m}]
.

Lemma 3.7.1
.

Let
\eta
be a non-trivial character of
\Gamma
of conductor
p^{n}
. Consider the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{G}(T_{p},U_{\infty})[\eta]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{G}(T_{p},U_{n-1})[\eta]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{G}(T_{p},U^{\prime}_{\infty})[\eta]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{G}(T_{p},U^{\prime}_{n-1})[\eta],}

where the horizontal maps are induced by
|\cdot|_{\eta}
. Then all the four maps are isomorphisms.

Proof.

Since
\Gamma
acts trivially on the quotient
U^{\prime}_{\infty}/U_{\infty}
(resp.
U^{\prime}_{n-1}/U_{n-1}
) and since
\eta
is non-trivial by assumption, we have
U_{\infty}[\eta]=U^{\prime}_{\infty}[\eta]
(resp.
U_{n-1}[\eta]=U^{\prime}_{n-1}[\eta]
), so the two vertical maps are isomorphisms. Hence, we only have to show that the bottom horizontal map is an isomorphism. By Lemmas
3.6.2
(1) and
3.6.4
(1), its domain has dimension
d^{+}
over
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}
, as well as for its codomain because
\eta\neq\mathds{1}
. Therefore, it is enough to check its injectivity, which easily follows from the fact that the projection map injects
(U^{\prime}_{\infty})_{\Gamma^{p^{n-1}}}
into
U^{\prime}_{n-1}
by
[
Kuz72
, Thm. 7.3]
.
∎

Remark 3.7.2
.

The
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}
-vector space
\Hom_{G}(T_{p},U_{n-1})[\eta]
in Lemma
3.7.1
can be identified with
\Hom_{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}(W_{p,\eta},U_{n-1}\otimes\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p})
, and hence with
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}\otimes_{E}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}((\rho\otimes\eta)^{*}(1))
.

Fix a eigenbasis
\omega_{p}^{+}=t_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge t_{d^{+}}
of
\det_{\mathcal{O}_{p}}T_{p}^{+}
for the action of
G_{p}
and let
\delta_{1},\ldots,\delta_{d^{+}}:G_{p}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{p}
be the corresponding characters. The number of
\delta_{i}
’s which are trivial is
f-e
, where
f=\dim\mathrm{H}^{0}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}},W_{p})
and
e=\dim\mathrm{H}^{0}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}},W_{p}^{-})
. We define two composite maps

(21)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}_{\omega_{p}^{+}}:{\bigwedge^{d^{+}}}\Hom_{G}(T_{p},U_{\infty})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bigwedge^{d^{+}}}\Hom_{G_{p}}(T^{+}_{p},U_{\infty,w})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bigwedge^{d^{+}}}\left(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{d^{+}}U^{\delta_{i}}_{\infty,w}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda,}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{str}}_{\omega_{p}^{+}}:{\bigwedge^{d^{+}}}\Hom_{G}(T_{p},U^{\prime}_{\infty})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bigwedge^{d^{+}}}\Hom_{G_{p}}(T^{+}_{p},U^{\prime}_{\infty,w})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bigwedge^{d^{+}}}\left(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{d^{+}}(U^{\prime}_{\infty,w})^{\delta_{i}}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{I}^{f-e},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wedge\mathrm{Loc}_{+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wedge\textrm{ev}_{\omega_{p}^{+}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wedge_{i}\Coleman^{\delta_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wedge\mathrm{Loc}^{\mathrm{str}}_{+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wedge\textrm{ev}_{\omega_{p}^{+}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wedge_{i}\widetilde{\Coleman}^{\delta_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the map
\textrm{ev}_{\omega_{p}^{+}}
is the natural map induced by the evaluation at
t_{1},\ldots,t_{d^{+}}
, the last maps are the one of Definitions
2.2.1
and
2.2.3
and
\mathcal{I}
is the invertible ideal of
\Lambda
introduced in Definition
2.2.3
for
\mathcal{O}=\mathcal{O}_{p}
. Once we fix a topological generator of
\Gamma
and thus an isomorphism
\Lambda\simeq\mathcal{O}_{p}[[T]]
,
\mathcal{I}^{f-e}
is nothing but
T^{-(f-e)}\Lambda\subseteq\Frac(\Lambda)
.

Lemma 3.7.3
.

Fix an eigenbasis
\omega_{p}^{+}
of
\det_{\mathcal{O}_{p}}T_{p}^{+}
for
G_{p}
and let
\omega=\Psi_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\Psi_{d^{+}}
be an element of
{\bigwedge^{d^{+}}}\Hom_{G}(T_{p},U_{\infty})
(resp. of
{\bigwedge^{d^{+}}}\Hom_{G}(T_{p},U^{\prime}_{\infty})
). Then for all non-trivial characters
\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}
of conductor
p^{n}
, the image
\theta
of
\omega
under
\mathscr{C}_{\omega_{p}^{+}}
(resp. under
\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{str}}_{\omega_{p}^{+}}
) satisfies

\eta(\theta)=\left(\frac{p^{n-1}}{\mathfrak{g}(\eta^{-1})}\right)^{d^{+}}\det(\rho^{+})(\sigma_{p}^{n})\det\left(\log_{p}|\Psi_{j}(t_{i})|_{\eta}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq d^{+}}.

Proof.

Let
\beta_{i}=\delta_{i}(\sigma_{p})
for all
1\leq i\leq d^{+}
. Note that
\det(\rho^{+})(\sigma_{p})=\prod_{i=1}^{d^{+}}\beta_{i}
. In the case where
\theta=\mathscr{C}_{\omega_{p}^{+}}(\omega)
, Lemma
2.1.3
shows that

\displaystyle\eta(\theta)

\displaystyle=\det\left(\frac{p^{n-1}}{\mathfrak{g}(\eta^{-1})}\beta_{i}^{n}\log_{p}|{\Psi}_{j}(t_{i})|_{\eta}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq d^{+}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{p^{n-1}}{\mathfrak{g}(\eta^{-1})}\right)^{d^{+}}\det(\rho^{+})(\sigma_{p}^{n})\det\left(\log_{p}|{\Psi}_{j}(t_{i})|_{\eta}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq d^{+}}

for any
\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}
of conductor
p^{n}>1
. Since
\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{str}}_{\omega_{p}^{+}}
extends
\mathscr{C}_{\omega_{p}^{+}}
, the case where
\theta=\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{str}}_{\omega_{p}^{+}}(\omega)
follows from the first one, noting that for non-trivial
\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}
, the maps
\eta\circ\mathscr{C}_{\omega_{p}^{+}}
and
\eta\circ\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{str}}_{\omega_{p}^{+}}
both factor through
\Hom_{G}(T_{p},U_{\infty})[\eta]=\Hom_{G}(T_{p},U_{\infty}^{\prime})[\eta]
and that they clearly coincide on it.
∎

Theorem 3.7.4
.

Fix a basis
\omega_{p}^{+}
of
\det_{\mathcal{O}_{p}}T_{p}^{+}
. The following conditions are equivalent:

(i)

X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
is a torsion
\Lambda
-module,

(ii)

\coker\left(\mathrm{Loc}_{+}\right)
is a torsion
\Lambda
-module,

(iii)

there exists a non-trivial character
\eta
of
{\Gamma}
of finite order such that
\Reg_{\omega_{p}^{+}}(\rho\otimes\eta)\neq 0
,

(iv)

for all but finitely many characters
\eta
of
\Gamma
of finite order, one has
\Reg_{\omega_{p}^{+}}(\rho\otimes\eta)\neq 0
.

Moreover, if these equivalent conditions hold and if
d^{+}>0
, then there exists linearly independent elements
\Psi_{1},\ldots,\Psi_{d^{+}}\in\Hom_{G}(T_{p},U_{\infty})
which only depend on
T_{p}
and there exists a generator
\theta^{\textrm{alg}}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
of the characteristic ideal of
X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
such that

(22)

\eta(\theta^{\textrm{alg}}_{\rho,\rho^{+}})=j\left(\frac{\tau(\eta)^{d^{-}}}{\tau(\rho\otimes\eta)}\right)\ \frac{p^{(n-1)\cdot d^{+}}}{\det(\rho^{-})(\sigma_{p}^{n})}\cdot\det\left(\log_{p}|\Psi_{j}(t_{i})|_{\eta}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq d^{+}},

for all non-trivial characters
\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}
of conductor
p^{n}
, where we have written
\omega_{p}^{+}=t_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge t_{d^{+}}
.

Proof.

We may assume without loss of generality that the basis
\omega_{p}^{+}=t_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge t_{d^{+}}
of
\det_{\mathcal{O}_{p}}T_{p}^{+}
is an eigenbasis for
G_{p}
. The equivalence of (i) and (ii) follows from Lemma
3.6.4
. We now show the equivalence of the three last statements and we will use Lemma
3.6.2
to identify the source and the target of
\mathrm{Loc}_{+}
with the respective
\Hom
’s, which are known to both be free of rank
d^{+}
over
\Lambda
by Lemma
3.6.4
. The statement (ii) is equivalent to the injectivity of
\mathrm{Loc}_{+}
, which in turn is equivalent to the injectivity of
\mathscr{C}_{\omega_{p}^{+}}
,
i.e.
, to the non-vanishing of
\theta_{1}^{\textrm{alg}}:=\mathscr{C}_{\omega_{p}^{+}}(\widetilde{\omega})
, where
\widetilde{\omega}=\tilde{\Psi}_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\tilde{\Psi}_{d^{+}}
is a
\Lambda
-basis of
\Hom_{G}(T_{p},U_{\infty})
. Moreover, if (ii) holds, then the characteristic ideal of
\coker(\mathrm{Loc}_{+})
is generated by
\theta_{1}^{\textrm{alg}}
. By Lemma
3.7.3
, for any character
\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}
of conductor
p^{n}>1
, one has

\eta(\theta_{1}^{\textrm{alg}})=\left(\frac{p^{n-1}}{\mathfrak{g}(\eta^{-1})}\right)^{d^{+}}\det(\rho^{+})(\sigma_{p}^{n})\det\left(\log_{p}|\tilde{\Psi}_{j}(t_{i})|_{\eta}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq d^{+}}.

Since the image of the basis
\tilde{\Psi}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\Psi}_{d^{+}}
under
|\cdot|_{\eta}
is a
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}
-basis of
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}\otimes_{E}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}((\rho\otimes\eta)^{*}(1))
by Lemma
3.7.1
and Remark
3.7.2
, the last
d^{+}\times d^{+}
-sized determinant is a non-zero multiple of
\Reg_{\omega_{p}^{+}}(\rho\otimes\eta)
. Therefore, by Weierstrass preparation theorem, one has
\theta_{1}^{\textrm{alg}}\neq 0
if and only if
\Reg_{\omega_{p}^{+}}(\rho\otimes\eta)\neq 0
for some character
\eta\neq\mathds{1}
, if and only if
\Reg_{\omega_{p}^{+}}(\rho\otimes\eta)\neq 0
for all but finitely many characters
\eta\neq\mathds{1}
. This shows the equivalence (ii)
\Leftrightarrow
(iii)
\Leftrightarrow
(iv).

Let us now assume (i)-(iv) and let
\theta_{2}^{\textrm{alg}}
be a generator of
\textrm{char}_{\Lambda}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{ur}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\infty},D_{p})^{\vee}
. By the exactness of the second row of the diagram (
20
) and by multiplicativity of characteristic ideals, the
p
-adic measure
\theta^{\textrm{alg}}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}:=\theta^{\textrm{alg}}_{1}\theta^{\textrm{alg}}_{2}
is a generator of
\textrm{char}_{\Lambda}X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
. If one moreover assumes
d^{+}>0
, then one can simply set

\Psi_{1}=\tau(\rho)^{-1}\cdot\theta_{N}\cdot\theta^{\textrm{alg}}_{2}\cdot\tilde{\Psi}_{1},\quad\Psi_{2}=\tilde{\Psi}_{2},\quad\ldots,\quad\Psi_{d^{+}}=\tilde{\Psi}_{d^{+}},

where
\theta_{N}
is as in the proof of Proposition
3.3.3
, and Formula (
22
)
follows by linearity from the above expression of
\eta(\theta_{1}^{\textrm{alg}})
.
∎

Corollary 3.7.5
.

There exists a
p
-stabilization
\rho^{+}
of
\rho
such that
X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
is a torsion
\Lambda
-module.

Proof.

It suffices to produce a
p
-stabilization
\rho^{+}
of
\rho
such that the condition (ii) of Theorem
3.7.4
holds. Since
\rho
is unramified at
p
,
W_{p}
splits as
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-module into a direct sum
E_{p}(\chi_{1})\oplus\ldots\oplus E_{p}(\chi_{d})
for certain one-dimensional characters
\chi_{1},\ldots,\chi_{d}
. Note that any subset
\{\chi_{i_{1}},\ldots,\chi_{i_{d^{+}}}\}
of size
d^{+}
of
\{\chi_{1},\ldots,\chi_{d}\}
yields a
p
-stabilization of
\rho
by letting
W_{p}^{+}=E_{p}(\chi_{i_{1}})\oplus\ldots\oplus E_{p}(\chi_{i_{d^{+}}})
, and the corresponding map
\mathrm{Loc}_{+}
is the composition:

\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q},\widecheck{T}_{p})\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f}}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}},\widecheck{T}_{p})=\bigoplus_{1\leq i\leq d}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f}}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}},\widecheck{T}_{p,i})\twoheadrightarrow\bigoplus_{1\leq k\leq d^{+}}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f}}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}},\widecheck{T}_{p,i_{k}}),

where
T_{p,i}=E_{p}(\chi_{d})\cap T_{p}
.

As the weak Leopoldt conjecture for
\rho
holds by Proposition
3.5.2
, the localization map
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q},\widecheck{T}_{p})\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f}}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}},\widecheck{T}_{p})
is injective (see
[
PR95
, §1.3]
). Moreover,
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q},\widecheck{T}_{p})
and
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f}}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}},\widecheck{T}_{p,i})
are free
\Lambda
-modules of respective ranks
d^{+}
and
1
(see Lemma
3.6.4
and diagram (
21
)). The corollary then follows from the following elementary fact: let
A\hookrightarrow B
be a map of free modules of respective ranks
d^{+}
and
d
over an integral domain
R
and assume that
B=B_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus B_{d}
where the
B_{i}
’s are all free of rank one. Then there exist
1\leq i_{1}<\ldots<i_{d^{+}}\leq d
such that the cokernel of the composite map
A\hookrightarrow B\twoheadrightarrow B_{i_{1}}\oplus\ldots\oplus B_{i_{d^{+}}}
is of
R
-torsion.
∎

3.8.
An
\mathcal{L}
-invariant for
(\rho,\rho^{+})

We define in this section an
\mathcal{L}
-invariant for
\rho
which depends on the choice of an admissible
p
-stabilization
W_{p}^{+}
of
W_{p}
. Its definition still makes sense for
\rho
ramified at
p
and it generalizes Gross’s
\mathcal{L}
-invariant (see §
6.3
). We put
\mathcal{U}={E_{p}}\otimes U_{0}
and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}={E_{p}}\otimes U^{\prime}_{0}
, where
U_{0}
(resp.
U^{\prime}_{0}
) is the formal
p
-adic completion of the group of global units (resp. of
p
-units) of
H
(see Notation
3.6.1
for
n=0
). Recall that the extension
K
of
{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
cut out by
\rho_{\big|G_{{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}}
is assumed to be contained in
E_{p}
. Recall also that
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{f,p}(\mathbb{Q},\widecheck{W}_{p})\simeq\Hom_{G}(W_{p},\mathcal{U}^{\prime})
. The
p
-adic valuation map and the
p
-adic logarithm map define two
G_{{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
-equivariant maps
\mathrm{ord}_{p}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\longrightarrow{E_{p}}
and
\log_{p}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\longrightarrow K\otimes{E_{p}}
where the
G_{{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
-action on the target of the first map is trivial. These maps give rise to two linear maps

\displaystyle\mathrm{ord}_{p}^{0}:

\displaystyle\Hom_{G}(W_{p},\mathcal{U}^{\prime})\longrightarrow\Hom_{G_{p}}(W_{p},{E_{p}})=\Hom(W^{0}_{p},{E_{p}})

\displaystyle\log_{p}:

\displaystyle\Hom_{G}(W_{p},\mathcal{U}^{\prime})\longrightarrow\Hom_{G_{p}}(W_{p},K\otimes{E_{p}})\simeq\Hom(W_{p},{E_{p}}),

where we have put
W^{0}_{p}=\mathrm{H}^{0}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}},W_{p})
and where the last isomorphism is induced by the internal multiplication
K\otimes{E_{p}}\rightarrow{E_{p}}
. Choose any
G_{{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
-stable complement
W^{-}_{p}
of
W^{+}_{p}
and let
W_{p}^{\pm,0}=\mathrm{H}^{0}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}},W^{\pm}_{p})
. The composition with restriction maps yields the following maps

\mathrm{ord}_{p}^{\pm,0}=\mathrm{res}_{W_{p}^{\pm,0}}\circ\mathrm{ord}_{p}^{0},\quad\log_{p}^{+}=\mathrm{res}_{W_{p}^{+}}\circ\log_{p},\quad\log^{-,0}=\mathrm{res}_{W_{p}^{-,0}}\circ\log_{p},

which can be combined in order to obtain two linear maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{G}(W_{p},\mathcal{U}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom(W_{p}^{0},{E_{p}})\bigoplus\Hom(W_{p}^{+},{E_{p}})=:Z.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ord}_{p}^{0}\oplus\log_{p}^{+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ord}_{p}^{+,0}\oplus\log_{p}^{-,0}\oplus\log_{p}^{+}}

Note that
W_{p}^{+}
is admissible if and only if the restriction of
\log_{p}^{+}
to
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q},\widecheck{W}_{p})=\Hom_{G}(W_{p},\mathcal{U})
is an isomorphism onto
\Hom(W^{+}_{p},{E_{p}})
. Therefore, the map
\mathrm{ord}_{p}^{0}\oplus\log_{p}^{+}
is an isomorphism.

Definition 3.8.1
.

Let
W^{+}_{p}\subseteq W_{p}
be an admissible
p
-stabilization of
\rho
. We define the
\mathcal{L}
-invariant attached to
(\rho,\rho^{+})
to be

\mathcal{L}(\rho,\rho^{+})=\det\left(\left(\mathrm{ord}_{p}^{+,0}\oplus\log_{p}^{-,0}\oplus\log_{p}^{+}\right)\circ\left(\mathrm{ord}_{p}^{0}\oplus\log_{p}^{+}\right)^{-1}\;\bigg|\;Z\;\right)\in{E_{p}}.

We now give equivalent and more useful definitions of
\mathcal{L}(\rho,\rho^{+})
. Fix bases
\omega_{p}^{+}=t_{1}^{+}\wedge\ldots\wedge t_{d^{+}}^{+}
and
\omega_{p}^{-,0}=t^{-}_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge t^{-}_{e}
of
\det_{E_{p}}W_{p}^{+}
and of
\det_{E_{p}}W^{-,0}_{p}
respectively. Then one may identify
Z
with
d^{+}+f
copies of
E_{p}
and
\mathcal{L}(\rho,\rho^{+})
satisfies

(\wedge\mathrm{ord}_{p}^{+,0})\wedge(\wedge\log_{p}^{-,0})\wedge(\wedge\log_{p}^{+})=\mathcal{L}(\rho,\rho^{+})\cdot(\wedge\mathrm{ord}_{p}^{0})\wedge(\wedge\log_{p}^{+})

in
{\bigwedge^{d^{+}+f}}\Hom(\Hom(W_{p},\mathcal{U}^{\prime}),E_{p})=\det_{E_{p}}\Hom(\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f},p}(\mathbb{Q},\widecheck{W}_{p}),E_{p})
.

The kernel of
\mathrm{ord}_{p}^{+,0}
contains
\Hom_{G}(W_{p},\mathcal{U})
and is of dimension
d^{+}+e
because
W_{p}^{+}
is admissible. Choose any basis
\psi_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\psi_{d^{+}}\wedge\psi^{\prime}_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\psi^{\prime}_{e}
of
\det_{E_{p}}\ker\left(\mathrm{ord}_{p}^{+,0}\right)
such that
\psi_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\psi_{d^{+}}
is a basis of
\det_{E_{p}}\Hom_{G}(W_{p},\mathcal{U})
, and define the following matrices with coefficients in
{E_{p}}
:

(23)

A^{\pm}=\left[\log_{p}\left(\psi_{j}(t^{\pm}_{i})\right)\right]_{i,j},\quad B^{\pm}=\left[\log_{p}\left(\psi^{\prime}_{j}(t^{\pm}_{i})\right)\right]_{i,j},\quad O^{-}=\left[\mathrm{ord}_{p}\left(\psi^{\prime}_{j}(t^{-}_{i})\right)\right]_{i,j}.

The square matrices
A^{+}
,
B^{-}
and
O^{-}
have respective sizes
d^{+}
,
e
and
e
. The determinant of
A^{+}
is non-zero and it is equal to
\Reg_{\omega_{p}^{+}}(\rho)
modulo
E^{\times}
if the basis
\psi_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\psi_{d^{+}}
is taken
E
-rational (see (
9
)). Also,
O^{-}
is invertible.

Lemma 3.8.2
.

\mathcal{L}(\rho,\rho^{+})
can be expressed as a quotient of determinants as follows:

\mathcal{L}(\rho,\rho^{+})=\dfrac{\det\begin{pmatrix}A^{+}&B^{+}\\
A^{-}&B^{-}\end{pmatrix}}{\det(A^{+})\cdot\det(O^{-})}.

Proof.

It is a simple computation of linear algebra.
∎

3.9.
Relation to Perrin-Riou’s and Benois’ theory

Let
M
be the Artin motive attached to
\rho
and let
\widecheck{M}=M^{*}(1)
be its dual. Then
\widecheck{M}
is a pure motive of weight
-2
over
\mathbb{Q}
which is crystalline at
p
and whose
p
-adic realization is the arithmetic dual
\widecheck{W_{p}}=W_{p}^{*}(1)
of
W_{p}
. We relate our Selmer group defined in §
3.2
to Perrin-Riou’s definition of the module of
p
-adic
L
-functions attached to
\widecheck{M}
given in
[
PR95
]
by using Benois’ interpretation in terms of Selmer complexes. It depends on the choice of a Galois stable
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-lattice
T_{p}
of
W_{p}
and of a regular subspace
D
of
\textbf{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\widecheck{W}_{p})
whose definition is first recalled.

Let
G_{p}=\Gal(K/{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
and assume as before that
K\subseteq E_{p}
. Let
t\in\textbf{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
be Fontaine’s
p
-adic period. The Dieudonné module
\textbf{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\widecheck{W}_{p})=\left(\widecheck{W}_{p}\otimes\textbf{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}\right)^{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
can be described as

(24)

\textbf{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\widecheck{W}_{p})\simeq\Hom_{G_{p}}\left(W_{p},t^{-1}K\otimes E_{p}\right)\simeq\Hom\left(W_{p},E_{p}\right),

where
G_{p}
acts on
t^{-1}K\otimes E_{p}
via
g\cdot(t^{-1}x\otimes y)=t^{-1}g(x)\otimes y
and the second isomorphism is induced by the internal multiplication
t^{-1}K\otimes E_{p}\simeq K\otimes E_{p}\longrightarrow E_{p}
. The crystalline Frobenius
\varphi
acts on
f\in\Hom\left(W_{p},E_{p}\right)
by
\varphi(f)(w)=p^{-1}f(\sigma_{p}^{-1}.w)
, where
\sigma_{p}\in G_{p}
is the arithmetic Frobenius at
p
.

Any
\varphi
-submodule
D
of
\textbf{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\widecheck{W}_{p})
gives rise to a regulator map given by the composition

(25)

r_{D}:\hbox to313.27pt{\vbox to20.36pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 156.6343pt\lower-9.1603pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

\lxSVG@closescope {{

where
\mathrm{loc}_{p}
is the localization at
p
, and
\log_{\mathrm{BK}}
is Bloch-Kato’s logarithm. The definition of
r_{D}
for a more general
p
-adic representation
V
involves the tangent space
\textbf{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(V)/\mathrm{Fil}^{0}
instead of
\textbf{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(V)
, but in our case, we have
\mathrm{Fil}^{0}=\{0\}
. The
\varphi
-module
D
is called regular whenever
r_{D}
is an isomorphism (see
[
Ben14a
, §4.1.3]
).

Lemma 3.9.1
.

Under the identification (
24
) any
\varphi
-submodule
D
of
\textbf{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\widecheck{W}_{p})
of
E_{p}
-dimension
d^{-}
can be uniquely written as
D=\Hom\left(W_{p}/W^{+}_{p},E_{p}\right)
where
W_{p}^{+}
is a
p
-stabilization of
W_{p}
, and any
p
-stabilization
W_{p}^{+}
of
W_{p}
defines a
\varphi
-submodule in this way. It is moreover regular if and only if
W_{p}^{+}
is admissible.

Proof.

The first claim is obvious. Let us prove the second claim and put
D=\Hom(W_{p}/W_{p}^{+},E_{p})
, where
W_{p}^{+}
is a
p
-stabilization of
W_{p}
. Under the identification (
24
) the composite map
\log_{\mathrm{BK}}\circ\mathrm{loc}_{p}
coincides with the composite map given in (
12
). Therefore,
r_{D}
coincides with the map
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q},\widecheck{W}_{p})\longrightarrow\Hom(W_{p}^{+},E_{p})
induced by the
p
-adic pairing (
3
), which, by definition, is an isomorphism if and only if
W_{p}^{+}
is admissible.
∎

Given a pure motive of weight
-2
whose
p
-adic realization
V
satisfies conditions
(C1-C5)
of
[
Ben14a
, §4.1.2]
and given a regular submodule
D
of
\textbf{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(V)
, Benois has defined an
\mathcal{L}
-invariant
\mathcal{L}(V,D)

[
Ben14a
, §4.1.4]
. It is not hard to see that
V=\widecheck{W}_{p}
satisfies the above-mentioned conditions: the first one follows from the finiteness of the ideal class group of
H
, the second one from the running assumption
\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{Q},W)=0
, the third and fourth ones from the unramifiedness assumption at
p
and from the semi-simplicity of
\rho(\sigma_{p})
, and the last one is true whenever there exists at least one regular submodule
D
of
\textbf{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\widecheck{W}_{p})
.

Lemma 3.9.2
.

Let
W_{p}^{+}
be an admissible
p
-stabilization of
W_{p}
, let
V=\widecheck{W}_{p}
be the
p
-adic realization of
\widecheck{M}
and let
D
be the regular submodule of
\textbf{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(V)
defined as in (
24
). Then

\mathcal{L}(\rho,\rho^{+})=(-1)^{e}\cdot\mathcal{L}(V,D),

where
\mathcal{L}(V,D)
is Benois’
\mathcal{L}
-invariant for
V
and
D
as defined in
[
Ben14a
, §4.1.4]
.

Proof.

It is shown in
[
DM22
, Thm. 2.15]
that
\mathcal{L}(V,D)=(-1)^{e}\cdot\dfrac{\det\begin{pmatrix}A^{+}&B^{+}\\
A^{-}&B^{-}\end{pmatrix}}{\det(A^{+})\det(O^{-})}
, where
A^{\pm}
,
B^{\pm}
,
O^{-}
are the matrices defined in §
3.8
. Therefore, one has
\mathcal{L}(\rho,\rho^{+})=(-1)^{e}\cdot\mathcal{L}(V,D)
by Lemma
3.8.2
.
∎

The main algebraic object in Perrin-Riou’s formulation of Iwasawa theory for a motive that is crystalline at
p
is the module of
p
-adic
L
-functions, introduced and studied in
[
PR95
, Chapter 2]
and later interpreted (and generalized) in
[
Ben14a
, §6.2.3]
in terms of Selmer complexes. Its definition only makes sense when
\mathcal{L}(V,D)\neq 0
and under the Weak Leopoldt conjecture for
V
and for
\widecheck{V}=V^{*}(1)
together with conditions
(C1-C5)
of
loc. cit.
. It is denoted by
\textbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},h}^{(\eta_{0})}(N,T)
in
loc. cit.
and it depends on the choice of a
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-stable lattice
T
of
V
, on the choice of a
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-lattice
N
of a regular submodule
D
of
\textbf{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(V)
and on a parameter
h>0
.

We consider here the case of the dual motive
\widecheck{M}
of
\rho
. More precisely, let
T_{p}
be a
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-stable lattice of
W_{p}
and let
W_{p}^{+}
be an admissible
p
-stabilization of
W_{p}
. We put
V=\widecheck{W}_{p}
,
T=\widecheck{T}_{p}
,
V^{-}=\widecheck{W}_{p}^{-}
and
T^{-}=\widecheck{T}_{p}^{-}
in this paragraph. Under the identification (
24
), we define a regular submodule of
\textbf{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(V)
by letting
D=\textbf{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(V^{-})=\Hom_{G_{p}}(W_{p}^{-},t^{-1}K\otimes E_{p})
(see Lemma
3.9.1
). Explicitly, Bloch-Kato’s logarithm map for
V^{-}
is the isomorphism

(26)

\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}},V^{-})=\Hom_{G_{p}}(W_{p}^{-},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times,1}\otimes E_{p})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\Hom_{G_{p}}(W_{p}^{-},t^{-1}K\otimes E_{p})=D

induced by the
p
-adic logarithm
\log_{p}:\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times,1}\hookrightarrow K\simeq t^{-1}K
, where
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times,1}
is the group of principal units of
K
. We define an
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-lattice of
D
by letting

N=\textbf{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(T^{-})=\Hom_{G_{p}}(T_{p}^{-},t^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{K}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{p}).

We also may set
h=1
in the definition of
\textbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},h}^{(\eta_{0})}(N,T)
since the
G_{{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
-representation
V
is the Tate twist of an unramified representation.

Proposition 3.9.3
.

Assume that
W_{p}^{+}
is admissible and that
\mathcal{L}(\rho,\rho^{+})\neq 0
. Then
X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
is of
\Lambda
-torsion, and we have
\textbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},h}^{(\eta_{0})}(N,T)=\textrm{char}_{\Lambda}X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
.

Proof.

Fix a topological generator
\gamma
of
\Gamma
and let

\mathcal{H}=\left\{\>f(\gamma-1)\ \big|\ f(X)\in E_{p}[[X]]\textrm{ is holomorphic on the open unit disc\>}\right\}

be the large Iwasawa algebra. Consider the complex of
\mathcal{H}
-modules
\textbf{R}\Gamma^{(\eta_{0})}_{\mathrm{Iw},h}(D,V)
defined in
[
Ben14a
, §6.1.2]
. Note that it is possible because we already checked conditions
(C1-5)
and because the Weak Leopoldt conjecture for
V
and
V^{*}(1)
holds by Proposition
3.5.2
. It is a Selmer complex in the sense of
[
Nek06
, (6.1)]
given by the following local conditions: at finite primes
\ell\neq p
we take the unramified condition, and at
p
we take the derived version of Perrin-Riou’s exponential map

\textrm{Exp}_{V,h}:(N\otimes\Lambda)\otimes_{\Lambda}\mathcal{H}\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}},\widecheck{T}_{p}^{-})\otimes_{\Lambda}\mathcal{H}\subseteq\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}},T)\otimes_{\Lambda}\mathcal{H}.

As explained in
[
PR94
, §4.1.3-5]
, the map
\textrm{Exp}_{V,h}
is induced by the inverse of Coleman’s isomorphism
\Coleman_{N}:\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f}}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}},\widecheck{T}_{p}^{-})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}N\otimes\Lambda
. Since
\mathcal{H}
is flat over
\Lambda
, the complex
\textbf{R}\Gamma^{(\eta_{0})}_{\mathrm{Iw},h}(D,V)
is a base change to
\mathcal{H}
of a Selmer complex
\textbf{R}\Gamma_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\rho,\rho^{+})
over
\Lambda
given by the unramified condition at
\ell\neq p
, and at
p
by the morphism of complexes
N\otimes\Lambda[-1]\longrightarrow\textbf{R}\Gamma_{\mathrm{Iw}}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}},T)
induced by
(\Coleman_{N})^{-1}
in degree 1. By
[
Ben14a
, Thm. 4]
, the
\Lambda
-module
\textbf{R}^{i}\Gamma_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\rho,\rho^{+})
vanishes when
i\neq 2
and it is of
\Lambda
-torsion for
i=2
. Moreover, as in
[
Ben14a
, §6.1.3.3]
we have a short exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coker(\mathrm{Loc}_{+}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\rho,\rho^{+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sha_{\infty}^{2}(\widecheck{T}_{p})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0,}

where
\mathrm{Loc}_{+}^{\prime}
is the localization map introduced in §
3.5
. It follows from the exactness of the first row of (
20
) and from Proposition
3.5.2
that
X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
is also of
\Lambda
-torsion, and that it shares the same characteristic ideal with
\textbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\rho,\rho^{+})
, the latter being equal to
\textbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},h}^{(\eta_{0})}(N,T)
by construction.
∎

Theorem 3.9.4
.

Assume that
W_{p}^{+}
is admissible and that
\mathcal{L}(\rho,\rho^{+})\neq 0
. If Conjecture
A
holds, then the
p
-part of Bloch-Kato’s conjecture (in the formulation of Fontaine and Perrin-Riou,
[
FPR94
, III, 4.5.2]
) holds for the Artin motive associated with
\rho
, that is,

(27)

\dfrac{L^{*}\left(\rho^{*},0\right)}{\Reg_{\omega_{\infty}^{+}}(\rho)}\,\sim_{p}\,\dfrac{\#\Sha({T}_{p})\cdot\prod_{\ell\neq p}\#\left(\mathrm{H}^{1}(I_{\ell},\widecheck{T}_{p})^{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}}}\right)_{\textrm{tors}}}{\#\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{Q},D_{p})},

where
a\sim_{p}b
means that
a
and
b
are equal up to a
p
-adic unit,
\Reg_{\omega_{\infty}^{+}}(\rho)
is computed with respect to
T_{p}
-optimal bases
\omega_{\infty}^{+}
and
\omega_{\mathrm{f}}
of
\det_{E}\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{R},W)
and
\det_{E_{\eta}}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\rho^{*}(1))
respectively,
\Sha(T_{p})
is the Tate-Shafarevitch group of
T_{p}

[
FPR94
, II,5.3.4]
and
I_{\ell}
is the absolute inertia group at
\ell
. In particular, when
p
does not divide the order of the image of
\rho
, one has

\dfrac{L^{*}\left(\rho^{*},0\right)}{\Reg_{\omega_{\infty}^{+}}(\rho)}\sim_{p}\#\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{p}[G]}(T_{p},\mathcal{O}_{p}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathscr{C}\!\ell(H)),

where
\mathscr{C}\!\ell(H)
is the ideal class group of
H
, the field out by
\rho
.

Proof.

Let
V=\widecheck{W}_{p}
and
T=\widecheck{T}_{p}
as before.
Assume Conjecture
A
for
(\rho,\rho^{+})
and consider the
p
-adic measure
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}^{\prime}
of Proposition
3.3.3
. Fix
T_{p}
-optimal bases
\omega_{p}^{+}
and
\omega_{\mathrm{f}}
of
\det_{E_{p}}W_{p}^{+}
and
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q},V)
respectively. Proposition
3.9.3
tells us that, under
IMC
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
, the
p
-adic analytic function
s\mapsto\kappa^{s}(\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}^{\prime})
is equal (up to a unit) to the one denoted
L_{\mathrm{Iw},h}(T,N,s)
in
[
Ben14a
, §6.2.3]
. Therefore,
[
Ben14a
, Cor. 2]
reads, with the notations of
loc. cit.
,

(28)

\frac{1}{e!}\dfrac{\diff^{e}}{\diff\!s^{e}}\kappa^{s}(\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}^{\prime})\bigg|_{s=0}\,\sim_{p}\,R_{V,D}(\omega_{T,N})\cdot\mathcal{L}(V,D)\cdot\mathcal{E}^{+}(V,D)\cdot\dfrac{\#\Sha({T}_{p})\cdot\textrm{Tam}^{0}_{\omega_{T}}(T)}{\#\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{Q},D_{p})\cdot\#\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{Q},\widecheck{D}_{p})}.

Since
T_{p}
is unramified, note that
\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{Q},\widecheck{D}_{p})=\{0\}
, as
\mathrm{H}^{0}(I_{p},\widecheck{D}_{p})=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{p}}(T_{p},\mu_{p^{\infty}}^{I_{p}})=\{0\}
. From Lemma
3.9.2
, we know that
\mathcal{L}(V,D)\sim_{p}\mathcal{L}(\rho,\rho^{+})
, and from the description in (
24
) of the
\varphi
-module
\textbf{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(V)
, it is not hard to see that
\mathcal{E}^{+}(V,D)=\mathcal{E}(\rho,\rho^{+})
.

By definition,
\textrm{Tam}^{0}_{\omega_{T}}(T)
is a product of local Tamagawa factors
\textrm{Tam}^{0}_{p,\omega_{T}}(T)\cdot\prod_{\ell\neq p}\textrm{Tam}^{0}_{\ell}(T)
whose recipe is given in
[
FPR94
, Chapitre I, §4.1]
. It is shown in
loc. cit.
that
\textrm{Tam}^{0}_{\ell}(T)=\#\left(\mathrm{H}^{1}(I_{\ell},\widecheck{T}_{p})^{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}}}\right)_{\textrm{tors}}
for every
\ell\neq p
.

It remains to determine the product
R_{V,D}(\omega_{T,N})\cdot\textrm{Tam}^{0}_{p,\omega_{T}}(T)
. Each term of this product depends on the choice of a basis of
\textbf{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(V)
, but the product does not. The term
R_{V,D}(\omega_{T,N})
also depends on the choice of
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-bases
\omega_{\mathrm{f}}
and
\omega_{N}
of
\det_{\mathcal{O}_{p}}\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{f}}^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T)
and
\det_{\mathcal{O}_{p}}N
respectively. Since
\omega_{p}^{+}
is
T_{p}
-optimal, it yields a basis
(\omega_{p}^{+})^{*}
of
\det_{\mathcal{O}_{p}}\textbf{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(T^{+})=\det_{\mathcal{O}_{p}}(T^{+}_{p},t^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{K}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{p})
. We take
\omega_{T}
to be the image of
\omega_{N}\otimes(\omega_{p}^{+})^{*}
under the natural map

\det\textbf{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(T^{-})\otimes\det\textbf{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(T^{+})\longrightarrow\det\textbf{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(T)\subseteq\det\textbf{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(V).

By definition,
R_{V,D}(\omega_{T,N})
is equal to the determinant of the map
r_{D}
of (
25
) with respect to
\omega_{\mathrm{f}}
,
\omega_{T}
and
\omega_{N}
. Lemma
3.9.1
and its proof then show that
R_{V,D}(\omega_{T,N})=\Reg_{\omega_{p}^{+}}(\rho)
. We also obtain from the definition that
\textrm{Tam}^{0}_{p,\omega_{T}}(T)\sim_{p}1
, using that
\omega_{T}
is a basis of
\det_{\mathcal{O}_{p}}\textbf{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(T)
.

We obtain (
27
) from
EZC
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
and (
28
) after simplification by
\Reg_{\omega_{p}^{+}}(\rho)\cdot\mathcal{L}(\rho,\rho^{+})\cdot\mathcal{E}(\rho,\rho^{+})\neq 0
.

We now explain how to simplify (
27
) in the case where
p
does not divide the order of
G
. Note that
\mathrm{H}^{0}(G,D_{p})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}(G,T_{p})=0
, as this last
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-module is killed by
\#G
. To see that the local Tamagawa numbers are all trivial, let us fix any prime
\ell\neq p
. By
[
Rub00
, Lem. 3.2 (ii) and Lemma 3.5 (ii-iii)]
, one has
\left(\mathrm{H}^{1}(I_{\ell},\widecheck{T}_{p})^{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}}}\right)_{\textrm{tors}}\simeq\mathcal{W}^{\sigma_{\ell}=1}
, where
\mathcal{W}
is the quotient of
\widecheck{D}_{p}^{I_{\ell}}
by its divisible part. But the action of
I_{\ell}
on
\widecheck{D}_{p}
factors through a finite group of prime-to-
p
order, so we must have
\mathcal{W}=0
. Finally, the description of
\Sha(T_{p})
in terms of class groups directly follows from the inflation-restriction exact sequence.
∎

Remark 3.9.5
.

The proof of Theorem
3.9.4
used Benois’ computation of the leading term of the characteristic series of a Selmer group, which is a generalization of Greenberg’s computation
[
Gre94
, Prop. 4]
to the case of non-critical and non-ordinary motives. A proof of (
28
) could possibly be adapted from Greenberg’s arguments, but extra work would be needed in order to relax the assumptions in
loc. cit.
, which force all the terms
\mathcal{E}^{+}(V,D)
,
\#\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{Q},D_{p})
,
\#\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{Q},\widecheck{D}_{p})
,
\textrm{Tam}^{0}_{\ell}(T)
(
\ell\neq p
) to be
p
-adic units.

Recall that
A_{\infty}^{\prime}=\varprojlim_{n}A_{n}^{\prime}
is the inverse limit over
n
of the
p
-split ideal class group of
H_{n}
and it has a structure of
\Lambda[G]
-module (see Notation
3.6.1
). We end this section with some applications of our results to the Gross-Kuz’min conjecture
[
Kuz72
,
Gro81
]
, also called “Gross’ finiteness conjecture” in
[
BKS17
, Thm. 1.1]
.

Conjecture 3.9.6

(Gross-Kuz’min conjecture)
.

The module of
\Gamma
-coinvariants of
A^{\prime}_{\infty}
is finite.

Our main contribution concerns the
\rho
-isotypic part of the Gross-Kuz’min conjecture which can be stated as follows.

Conjecture 3.9.7

(
\rho
-part of the Gross-Kuz’min conjecture)
.

The module of
\Gamma
-coinvariants of
\Hom_{G}(T_{p},A_{\infty}^{\prime})
is finite.

Conjecture
3.9.7
is equivalent to the vanishing of
\Hom_{G}(W_{p},A_{\infty}^{\prime}\otimes{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})_{\Gamma}
, so it does not depend on the choice of the lattice
T_{p}
. In particular, Conjecture
3.9.6
is true if and only if Conjecture
3.9.7
is true for all
p
-adic representations
\rho
of
\Gal(H/\mathbb{Q})
.

Theorem 3.9.8
.

Let
f=\dim\mathrm{H}^{0}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}},W_{p})
.

(1)

Let
W_{p}^{+}
be any admissible
p
-stabilization of
W_{p}
such that
\mathcal{L}(\rho,\rho^{+})\neq 0
. Any generator of the characteristic ideal of
X_{\infty}(\rho,\rho^{+})
belongs to
\mathcal{A}^{e}\backslash\mathcal{A}^{e+1}
, where
\mathcal{A}\subseteq\Lambda
is the augmentation ideal and
e=\dim\mathrm{H}^{0}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}},W_{p}^{-})
.

(2)

If there exists at least one admissible
p
-stabilization
W_{p}^{+}
of
W_{p}
such that
\mathcal{L}(\rho,\rho^{+})\neq 0
, then Conjecture
3.9.7
holds.

(3)

If
f=0
and if the
\rho
-isotypic component of Leopoldt’s conjecture for
H
and
p
holds (see (
13
)), then Conjecture
3.9.7
holds as well.

(4)

If
f\leq 1
and
d^{+}\leq 1
, then Conjecture
3.9.7
holds.

Proof.

The first statement follows from Proposition
3.9.3
and from
[
Ben14a
, Thm. 5 (i)]
. For the three other statements, first note that, by the exactness of the third row of (
20
), by Proposition
3.5.2
(1) and by Lemma
3.6.2
(ii), the existence of a
p
-stabilization
\rho^{+}
such that
X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{str}}(\rho,\rho^{+})
has finite
\Gamma
-coinvariants immediately implies Conjecture
3.9.7
. Therefore, claim (2) follows from (1) and from Lemma
3.2.2
.

Consider (3) and assume that
f=0
and that the map in (
13
) is injective for
\eta=\mathds{1}
. By Lemma
3.1.4
, there exists an admissible
p
-stabilization
\rho^{+}
of
\rho
, and since
f=0
, one must have
e=0
as well. Therefore,
\mathcal{L}(\rho,\rho^{+})=1
by Lemma
3.8.2
, so (3) follows from (2).

Let us prove (4), and assume that
f,d^{+}\leq 1
. We may assume
\rho
irreducible. When
d^{+}=1
, it is easy to produce a motivic
p
-stabilization
\rho^{+}
such that
e=0
, so
\mathcal{L}(\rho,\rho^{+})=1
(take
W_{p}^{+}
containing
\mathrm{H}^{0}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}},W_{p})
). Since every motivic
p
-stabilization is automatically admissible by Lemma
3.1.3
, Conjecture
3.9.7
follows in this case from (2). The case where
d^{+}=0
follows from
[
Gro81
, Prop. 2.13]
, once we have checked that
\mathcal{L}(\rho,\rho^{+})
generalizes Gross’s regulator for
\rho^{+}=0
(see §
6.3
for details).
∎

3.10.
Changing the
p
-stabilization

Let
t_{1},\ldots,t_{d}
be an eigenbasis of
T_{p}
for
\sigma_{p}
. We may define a basis
\{\omega_{p,\alpha}\ |\ \alpha\in I\}
of
{\bigwedge}^{d^{+}}W_{p}
by letting
\omega_{p,\alpha}^{+}=t_{i_{1}}\wedge\ldots\wedge t_{i_{d^{+}}}
, where
\alpha=(1\leq i_{1}<\ldots<i_{d^{+}}\leq d)
runs over the set
I
of strictly increasing sequences of
d^{+}
integers between
1
and
d
. For each
\alpha\in I
,
\omega_{p,\alpha}^{+}
defines a
T_{p}
-optimal basis of a
p
-stabilization
(\rho_{\alpha}^{+},W_{p,\alpha}^{+})
of
W_{p}
.

Let
\omega_{p}^{+}\in{\bigwedge}^{d^{+}}W_{p}
be a
T_{p}
-optimal eigenbasis of a given
p
-stabilization
(\rho^{+},W_{p}^{+})
of
W_{p}
. Write
\omega_{p}^{+}
as
\sum_{\alpha\in I}c_{\alpha}\cdot\omega_{p,\alpha}^{+}
for
c_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{O}_{p}
. Writing
\omega_{p}^{+}
as a pure tensor and expanding in the eigenbasis
t_{1},\ldots,t_{d}
shows that, for any
\alpha\in I
, we have
c_{\alpha}=0
unless
\rho^{+}(\sigma_{p})
and
\rho^{+}_{\alpha}(\sigma_{p})
share the same list of eigenvalues. Thus, we have in particular
\mathcal{E}(\rho,\rho^{+})=\mathcal{E}(\rho,\rho^{+}_{\alpha})
,
\det(\rho^{\pm})(\sigma_{p})=\det(\rho_{\alpha}^{\pm})(\sigma_{p})
and
e:=\dim\mathrm{H}^{0}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}},W_{p}^{-})=\dim\mathrm{H}^{0}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}},W_{p,\alpha}^{-})
for all
\alpha\in I_{\rho^{+}}=\{\alpha\in I\,/\,c_{\alpha}\neq 0\}
.

Proposition 3.10.1
.

If
EX
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}_{\alpha}}
holds for all
\alpha\in I_{\rho^{+}}
, then
EX
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
holds as well, and
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}=\sum_{\alpha\in I_{\rho^{+}}}c_{\alpha}\cdot\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}_{\alpha}}
.

Proof.

Assume that
EX
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}_{\alpha}}
holds for all
\alpha\in I_{\rho^{+}}
. For every character
\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}
, the rule
\omega_{p}^{+}\mapsto\Reg_{\omega_{p}^{+}}(\rho\otimes\eta)
(where the
p
-adic regulator is computed in a fixed basis
\omega_{\mathrm{f},\eta}
of
\det_{E}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}((\rho\otimes\eta)^{*}(1))
) defines a
E_{p}
-linear map

{\bigwedge}^{d^{+}}W_{p}\longrightarrow E_{p,\eta},
so we have

\Reg_{\omega_{p}^{+}}(\rho\otimes\eta)=\sum_{\alpha\in I_{\rho^{+}}}c_{\alpha}\cdot\Reg_{\omega^{+}_{p,\alpha}}(\rho\otimes\eta).

Therefore, the element
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}\in\Frac(\Lambda)
defined as
\sum_{\alpha\in I_{\rho^{+}}}c_{\alpha}\cdot\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}_{\alpha}}
satisfies

\eta(\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}})=\sum_{\alpha\in I_{\rho^{+}}}c_{\alpha}\cdot\eta(\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}_{\alpha}})=M_{\rho,\eta}\cdot\sum_{\alpha\in I_{\rho^{+}}}c_{\alpha}\cdot\frac{\Reg_{\omega^{+}_{p,\alpha}}(\rho\otimes\eta)}{\det(\rho_{\alpha}^{-})(\sigma_{p}^{n}))}=M_{\rho,\eta}\cdot\frac{\Reg_{\omega^{+}_{p}}(\rho\otimes\eta)}{\det(\rho^{-})(\sigma_{p}^{n}))}

for all non-trivial characters
\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}
of conductor
p^{n}
, where we have put
M_{\rho,\eta}=\frac{\tau(\eta)^{d^{-}}}{\tau(\rho\otimes\eta)}\ \frac{L^{*}\left((\rho\otimes\eta)^{*},0\right)}{\Reg_{\omega_{\infty}^{+}}(\rho\otimes\eta)}
. Therefore,
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
satisfies the interpolation property of
EX
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
. Since it has no pole except maybe at
\mathds{1}
by construction, we have shown that
EX
{}_{\rho,\rho^{+}}
is valid.
∎

Remark 3.10.2
.

Conjecture
A
satisfies the following “
p
-adic Artin formalism”: if
\rho=\rho_{1}\bigoplus\rho_{2}
, and if
\rho^{+}
is a
p
-stabilization of
\rho
which splits into a sum of two
p
-stabilizations
\rho_{1}^{+}
and
\rho_{2}^{+}
of
\rho_{1}
and
\rho_{2}
respectively, then the validity of
IMC
for any two pairs in
\left\{(\rho,\rho^{+}),(\rho_{1},\rho_{1}^{+}),(\rho_{2},\rho_{2}^{+})\right\}
implies the validity of
IMC
for the third pair, in which case
\theta_{\rho,\rho^{+}}=\theta_{\rho_{1},\rho_{1}^{+}}\cdot\theta_{\rho_{2},\rho_{2}^{+}}
. Also, either
EX
or
EZC
for both
(\rho_{1},\rho_{1}^{+})
and
(\rho_{2},\rho_{2}^{+})
implies the same statement for
(\rho,\rho^{+})
. However,
\rho^{+}
needs not split in general even if
\rho
is reducible. Therefore, Conjecture
A
for
\rho
(and varying
\rho^{+}
) appears to be stronger than Conjecture
A
for
\rho_{1}
and
\rho_{2}
taken together.

4.
Conjectures on Rubin-Stark elements

4.1.
The Rubin-Stark conjecture

Let
H/\mathbb{Q}
be a Galois extension which is unramified at
p
and let
\chi
be a non-trivial
E
-valued character of
\Gal(H/k)
, where
k/\mathbb{Q}
is an intermediate extension of
H/\mathbb{Q}
. Denote by
L=H^{\ker\chi}
be the field cut out by
\chi
and by
\Delta
the Galois group of the abelian extension
L/k
. We fix for the moment an integer
n\geq 0
, and we put
L_{n}=L\mathbb{Q}_{n}
and
\Delta_{n}=\Gal(L_{n}/k)\simeq\Delta\times\Gamma_{n}
. Consider the following finite sets of places of
k
:

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle=S_{\infty}(k)\cup S_{\textrm{ram}}(L/k),

\displaystyle S^{\prime}

\displaystyle=S\cup S_{p}(k),

\displaystyle V^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\{v\in S^{\prime}\ \big|\ \chi(\Delta_{v})=1\}=\{v_{\infty,1},\ldots,v_{\infty,d^{+}},v_{p,1},\ldots,v_{p,f}\},

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle=V^{\prime}\backslash S_{p}(k)=\{v_{\infty,1},\ldots,v_{\infty,d^{+}}\}.

Fix once and for all a place
w_{\infty,i}
(resp.
w_{p,i}
) of
L_{n}
above
v_{\infty,i}
(resp. above
v_{p,i}
) for all indexes
i
. Recall from (
15
) and (
16
) that we have a
\Delta_{n}
-equivariant isomorphism

\lambda_{{L_{n}},S^{\prime}}:\mathbb{R}\mathcal{O}_{{L_{n}},S^{\prime}}^{\times}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{R}X_{{L_{n}},S^{\prime}},\qquad a\mapsto-\sum_{w|v\in S^{\prime}}\log|a|_{w}w,

where
\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{{L_{n}},S^{\prime}}
is the ring of
S^{\prime}
-units of
L_{n}
. For any character
\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}_{n}=\Hom(\Gamma_{n},\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\times})
, the order of vanishing of the
S^{\prime}
-truncated
L
-function of
(\chi\otimes\eta)^{-1}
is, by
[
Tat84
, Chapter I, Proposition 3.4]
,

r:=\mathrm{ord}_{s=0}L_{S^{\prime}}((\chi\otimes\eta)^{-1},s)=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}(e_{\chi\otimes\eta}\mathbb{C}\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{L_{n},{S^{\prime}}})=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}(e_{\chi\otimes\eta}\mathbb{C}X_{L_{n},S^{\prime}})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcc}d^{+}&\textrm{if}&\eta\neq 1\\
d^{+}+f&\textrm{if}&\eta=1,\end{array}\right.

where
e_{\chi\otimes\eta}=(\#\Delta_{n})^{-1}\sum_{\delta\in\Delta_{n}}(\chi\otimes\eta)^{-1}(\delta)\delta=e_{\chi}\cdot e_{\eta}
denotes the idempotent associated with
\chi\otimes\eta
. Thus, the limit
L^{*}_{S^{\prime}}({(\chi\otimes\eta)^{-1}},0):=\lim_{s\rightarrow 0}L_{S^{\prime}}(\chi,s)/s^{r}\in\mathbb{C}
is well-defined and non-zero.

Definition 4.1.1
.

The
\chi
-part of the Rubin-Stark elements

\varepsilon^{\chi}_{n}\in{\bigwedge}^{d^{+}}_{\mathbb{C}[\Gamma_{n}]}e_{\chi}\mathbb{C}\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{L_{n},S^{\prime}}\quad(n\geq 1),\qquad\left(\textrm{resp. }\ u^{\chi}\in{\bigwedge}^{d^{+}+f}_{\mathbb{C}}e_{\chi}\mathbb{C}\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{L,S^{\prime}}\quad(n=0)\right),

is defined to be the inverse image under
\lambda_{{L_{n}},S^{\prime}}
of

\left(\sum_{\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}_{n}}L^{*}_{S^{\prime}}({(\chi\otimes\eta)^{-1}},0)e_{\chi\otimes\eta}\right)\cdot\bigwedge_{w}w\in{\bigwedge}^{r}_{\mathbb{C}[\Gamma_{n}]}e_{\chi}\mathbb{C}Y_{L_{n},S^{\prime}}={\bigwedge}^{r}_{\mathbb{C}[\Gamma_{n}]}e_{\chi}\mathbb{C}X_{L_{n},S^{\prime}},

where
w
runs through
\{w_{\infty,1},\ldots,w_{\infty,d^{+}}\}
(resp. through
\{w_{\infty,1},\ldots,w_{p,f}\}
).
Note that the last equality follows from our assumption that
\chi
is non-trivial.

Remark 4.1.2
.

It will be convenient to see the
\chi
-part of the Rubin-Stark elements as
p
-units of
H_{n}
via the equality
e_{\chi}\mathbb{C}\mathcal{O}_{L_{n},S^{\prime}}^{\times}=e_{\chi}\mathbb{C}\mathcal{O}_{H_{n}}[\tfrac{1}{p}]^{\times}
. On the other hand, the
L
-series
L_{S^{\prime}}((\chi\otimes\eta)^{-1},s)
coincides with
L_{\{p\}}((\chi\otimes\eta)^{-1},s)
for
\chi=\mathds{1}
and with
L((\chi\otimes\eta)^{-1},s)
for
\chi\neq\mathds{1}
.

The Rubin-Stark conjecture over
\mathbb{Q}

[
Rub96
, Conjecture A’]
implies the following conjecture.

Conjecture 4.1.3

(Rubin-Stark conjecture for
\chi
: algebraicity statement)
.

One has

\varepsilon_{n}^{\chi}\in{\bigwedge}^{d^{+}}_{E[\Gamma_{n}]}e_{\chi}E\mathcal{O}_{H_{n}}[\tfrac{1}{p}]^{\times}\quad(n\geq 1),\qquad\textrm{resp. }\ u^{\chi}\in{\bigwedge}^{d^{+}+f}_{E}e_{\chi}E\mathcal{O}_{H}[\tfrac{1}{p}]^{\times}\quad(n=0).

By means of the isomorphism
j:\mathbb{C}\simeq\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}
, one may see the
\chi
-part of Rubin-Stark elements as living in the top exterior algebra of
e_{\chi}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}\mathcal{O}_{H_{n}}[\tfrac{1}{p}]^{\times}
. For
R=\mathcal{O}_{p}[\Gamma_{n}]
or
R=\Lambda
and for any finitely generated
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-free
R
-module
M
, let

{\bigcap}^{r}_{R}M:=\left({\bigwedge}^{r}_{R}M^{*}\right)^{*}\hookrightarrow{\bigwedge}^{r}_{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}\otimes R}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p}\otimes M

be the (
r
-th order) exterior bi-dual of
M
, where we have put
(-)^{*}=\Hom_{R}(-,R)
(see
[
BKS16
, §4]
and
[
BS22
, Appendix B]
for its basic properties). Note that the canonical map
{\bigwedge}^{r}_{R}M\longrightarrow{\bigcap}^{r}_{R}M
is an isomorphism when
M
is
R
-projective.

Recall that we denoted by
U_{n}
(resp.
U_{n}^{\prime}
) the
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-span of the pro-
p
completion of the group of units (resp. of
p
-units) of
H_{n}
(Notation
3.6.1
). We omit the index when
n=0
. Note that
U_{n}^{\prime}
is torsion-free because
p
is unramified in
H
. The Rubin-Stark conjecture over
\mathbb{Z}

[
Rub96
, Conjecture B’]
implies the following conjecture.

Conjecture 4.1.4

(Rubin-Stark conjecture for
\chi
:
p
-integrality statement)
.

One has

\varepsilon_{n}^{\chi}\in{\bigcap}^{d^{+}}_{\mathcal{O}_{p}[\Gamma_{n}]}U_{n}^{\prime}\quad(n\geq 1),\qquad\textrm{resp. }\ u^{\chi}\in{\bigwedge}^{d^{+}+f}_{\mathcal{O}_{p}}U^{\prime}\quad(n=0).

Recall that, if
\varphi
is a linear form on a
R
-module
M
(for a commutative ring
R
), and if
t\geq 1
is an integer, then
\varphi
induces a
R
-linear map
\varphi:{\bigwedge}^{t}_{R}M\longrightarrow{\bigwedge}^{t-1}_{R}M
which sends
m_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge m_{t}
to
\sum_{i=1}^{t}(-1)^{i-1}m_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge m_{i-1}\wedge m_{i+1}\wedge\ldots\wedge m_{t}
. More generally,
s
linear forms
\varphi_{1},\ldots\varphi_{s}
on
M
with
s\leq t
induce a
R
-linear map

\bigwedge_{1\leq i\leq s}\varphi_{i}:{\bigwedge}^{t}_{R}M\longrightarrow{\bigwedge}^{t-s}_{R}M

given by
m\mapsto\varphi_{s}\circ\ldots\circ\varphi_{1}(m)
.

We take
n=0
for the rest of this section and for
1\leq i\leq f
we consider the
p
-adic valuation
\mathrm{ord}_{w_{p,i}}:\mathbb{C}\mathcal{O}_{H}[\tfrac{1}{p}]^{\times}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
induced by the place
{w_{p,i}}
. By
[
San14
, Prop. 3.6]
, the induced map

\bigwedge_{1\leq i\leq f}\mathrm{ord}_{w_{p,i}}:{\bigwedge}_{\mathbb{C}}^{d^{+}+f}\mathbb{C}\mathcal{O}_{H}[\tfrac{1}{p}]^{\times}\longrightarrow{\bigwedge}_{\mathbb{C}}^{d^{+}}\mathbb{C}\mathcal{O}_{H}[\tfrac{1}{p}]^{\times}

sends
u^{\chi}
on the Rubin-Stark element

(29)

\xi^{\chi}\in{\bigwedge}_{\mathbb{C}}^{d^{+}}\mathbb{C}\mathcal{O}_{H}[\tfrac{1}{p}]^{\times}

defined as the inverse image under
\lambda_{L,S}
of
L^{*}(\chi^{-1},0)e_{\chi}\cdot w_{\infty,1}\wedge\ldots\wedge w_{\infty,d^{+}}
. If Conjecture
4.1.3
or Conjecture
4.1.4
holds for
u^{\chi}
, then the corresponding statement for
\xi^{\chi}
is also true.

4.2.
Iwasawa-theoretic conjectures

We assume in this section that
\chi
is non-trivial and of prime-to-
p
order. The idempotent
e_{\chi}
has coefficients in
\mathcal{O}_{p}
and the
\chi
-part
M^{\chi}
and the
\chi
-quotient
M_{\chi}
of an
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-module
M
(see §
2.2
) both coincide with
e_{\chi}M
. Moreover,
\chi
is residually non-trivial, a fact which is used in (
30
) and, therefore, in the formulation of Conjectures
4.2.1
,
4.2.2
and
4.2.3
.

We let the integer
n\geq 0
of last section vary and we assume Conjecture
4.1.4
for every
n
. As explained in
[
BKS17
, 3B2]
the family
(\varepsilon_{n}^{\chi})_{n\geq 1}
is norm-compatible, so it defines an element

(30)

\varepsilon_{\infty}^{\chi}\in\varprojlim_{n}{\bigcap}^{d^{+}}_{\mathcal{O}_{p}[\Gamma_{n}]}(U_{n}^{\prime})^{\chi}={\bigcap}^{d^{+}}_{\Lambda}(U_{\infty}^{\prime})^{\chi}={\bigwedge}^{d^{+}}_{\Lambda}(U_{\infty}^{\prime})^{\chi}.

Here, the first identification follows from
[
BS22
, Cor. B.5]
and the second one from the fact that
(U_{\infty}^{\prime})^{\chi}=\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw},\mathrm{f},p}(\mathbb{Q},\widecheck{T}_{p})
which is free (of rank
d^{+}
) over
\Lambda
by the results of §
3.6
. The following conjecture is taken from
[
BKS17
, Conj. 3.14]
and should be thought as a cyclotomic Iwasawa main conjecture for
\chi
. Let us mention that this conjecture may also be formulated for other
{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
-extensions of
k
than the cyclotomic one.

Conjecture 4.2.1

(
IMC
χ
)
.

We have

\textrm{char}_{\Lambda}\left({\bigwedge}^{d^{+}}(U_{\infty}^{\prime})^{\chi}\right)\big/\left(\Lambda\cdot\varepsilon_{\infty}^{\chi}\right)=\mathcal{A}^{f}\cdot\textrm{char}_{\Lambda}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})_{\chi},

where
\mathcal{A}
is the augmentation ideal of
\Lambda
and
A_{\infty}^{\prime}
is the inverse limit over
n\geq 0
of the
p
-split ideal class groups of
H_{n}
(see Notation
3.6.1
).

Since
{\bigwedge}^{d^{+}}(U_{\infty}^{\prime})^{\chi}
is free of rank one over
\Lambda
, Conjecture
4.2.1
implies immediately the non-vanishing of
\varepsilon_{\infty}^{\chi}
, as well as the following conjecture:

Conjecture 4.2.2

(
wEZC
χ
)
.

We have
\varepsilon_{\infty}^{\chi}\in\mathcal{A}^{f}\cdot{\bigwedge}^{d^{+}}(U_{\infty}^{\prime})^{\chi}
.

This is
[
BS22
, Conj. 2.7]
for the cyclotomic extension (and a more general number field
k
), where it is referred to as the Exceptional Zero Conjecture for Rubin-Stark elements. Assume Conjecture
4.2.2
and fix
\gamma
a topological generator of
\Gamma
. Following
loc. cit.
we now reformulate the (cyclotomic) Iwasawa-theoretic Mazur-Rubin-Sano Conjecture for
(\chi,S,V^{\prime})
in terms of the element
\kappa_{\infty,\gamma}\in{\bigwedge}^{d^{+}}(U_{\infty}^{\prime})^{\chi}
which satisfies

\varepsilon_{\infty}^{\chi}=(\gamma-1)^{f}\cdot\kappa_{\infty,\gamma}.

For all
1\leq i\leq f
, let
\rec_{w_{p,i}}:L^{\times}\longrightarrow\Gal((\mathbb{Q}_{\infty}L)_{w_{p,i}}/L_{w_{p,i}})\simeq\Gamma
be the local reciprocity map for
L
at
{w_{p,i}}
. We still denote by
\rec_{w_{p,i}}
the induced
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-homomorphism

\rec_{w_{p,i}}:(U^{\prime})^{\chi}=e_{\chi}(\mathcal{O}_{p}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{L,S^{\prime}}^{\times})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{p}\otimes\Gamma\simeq\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{A}^{2}.

Conjecture 4.2.3

(
MRS
χ
)
.

Conjecture
4.2.2
holds true, and if we let
\kappa_{\gamma}\in{\bigwedge}^{d^{+}}_{\mathcal{O}_{p}}(U^{\prime})^{\chi}
be the bottom layer of
\kappa_{\infty,\gamma}
, then the map

\bigwedge_{1\leq i\leq f}\rec_{w_{p,i}}:{\bigwedge}^{d^{+}+f}_{\mathcal{O}_{p}}(U^{\prime})^{\chi}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{f}/\mathcal{A}^{f+1}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{p}}{\bigwedge}^{d^{+}}_{\mathcal{O}_{p}}(U^{\prime})^{\chi}

sends
u^{\chi}
to
(-1)^{d^{+}\cdot f}\cdot(\#\Delta)^{-f}\cdot(\gamma-1)^{f}\otimes\kappa_{\gamma}
.

Remark 4.2.4
.

As first noted in
[
BS22
, Rem. 2.10 (i)]
, one may prove as in
[
BH21
, Prop. 3.13]
that Conjecture
4.2.3
is equivalent to
[
BKS17
, Conj. 4.2, MRS(
H_{\infty}/k
,
S
,
\emptyset
,
\chi
,
V^{\prime}
)]
. As for Conjecture
4.1.4
, taking
T=\emptyset
(in the notations of
[
Rub96
,
BKS17
]
) is allowed because the
{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
-module
U^{\prime}_{n}
is torsion-free for all
n\geq 0
. Lastly, note that, while the definition of Rubin-Stark elements depends on how we ordered the places
v_{p,1},\ldots,v_{p,f}
of
V^{\prime}\backslash V
and on the choice of
w_{p,i}
above
v_{p,i}
, the validity of all the conjectures of this section does not depend on these choices.

5.
Monomial representations

5.1.
Induced representations

Let
\rho
be a monomial representation and fix an isomorphism
\rho\simeq\mathrm{Ind}_{k}^{\mathbb{Q}}\chi
over
E
, where
\chi:\Gal(H/k)\longrightarrow E^{\times}
is a non-trivial character. We do not assume yet that
\chi
has order prime to
p
. The underlying space of
\rho
is then equal to
W=E[G]\otimes_{E[\Gal(H/k)]}E(\chi)
, where
E(\chi)
is a
E
-line on which
\Gal(H/k)
acts via
\chi
and the tensor product follows the rule
gh\otimes 1=g\otimes\chi(h)
for all
g\in G,h\in\Gal(H/k)
. The (left)
G
-action on
W
is given by
g\cdot(g^{\prime}\otimes 1)=gg^{\prime}\otimes 1
for all
g,g^{\prime}\in G
. By Frobenius reciprocity we have
\rho\otimes\eta\simeq\mathrm{Ind}_{k}^{\mathbb{Q}}(\chi\otimes\eta)
for any
\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}
, where we still denoted by
\eta
its restriction to
G_{k}
. We assume throughout §
5
that the
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-lattice
T_{p}
of
W_{p}
is

T_{p}=\mathcal{O}[G]\otimes_{\mathcal{O}[\Gal(H/k)]}\mathcal{O}(\chi),

so that the family
(g\otimes 1)_{g\in G}
generates
T_{p}
over
\mathcal{O}_{p}
.

Lemma 5.1.1
.

(1)

Given any
\mathcal{O}_{p}[G]
-module
M
, there is a canonical isomorphism

\Hom_{G}(T_{p},M)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{M}^{\chi},\qquad\psi\mapsto\psi(1\otimes 1),

where
{M}^{\chi}
denotes the
\chi
-part of
M
, seen as a
\Gal(H/k)
-module. In the same fashion, for any
\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}
and for any
E_{\eta}[G_{\mathbb{Q}}]
-module
M
, there is a canonical isomorphism
\Hom_{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}(W_{\eta},M)\simeq e_{\chi\otimes\eta}M={M}^{\chi\otimes\eta}
.

(2)

Given any
\mathcal{O}_{p}[G]
-module
M
, the module
\Hom_{G}(M,D_{p})
is canonically isomorphic to the Pontryagin dual
\left(M_{\chi}\right)^{\vee}
of the
\chi
-quotient of
M
.

Proof.

The first statement is straightforward to check, so we only prove the second one. Once we have fixed a generator of the different of
\mathcal{O}_{p}
over
{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
, the
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-module
\left(M_{\chi}\right)^{\vee}
can be identified with
\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{p}}(M_{\chi},E_{p}/\mathcal{O}_{p})
as in §
3.2
. On the other hand, since induction and co-induction functors over finite groups coincide,
D_{p}
can be described as the
\mathcal{O}_{p}
-module of maps
f:G\longrightarrow E_{p}/\mathcal{O}_{p}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{p}(\chi)
satisfying
f(hg)=h\cdot f(g)
for all
h\in\Gal(H/k)
and
g\in G
, the left
G
-action being
(g\cdot f)(g^{\prime})=f(g^{\prime}g)
. Therefore, the map
F\mapsto(m\mapsto F(m)(1\otimes 1))
identifies
\Hom_{G}(M,D_{p})
with
\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{p}}(M_{\chi},E_{p}/\mathcal{O}_{p})
.
∎

Notation 5.1.2
.

For any
\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}
, for any module
M
as in Lemma
5.1.1
(1) and for any
m\in M
, we let
\psi_{m}
be the element of
\Hom_{G}(T_{p},M)
(resp. of
\Hom_{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}(W_{\eta},M)
) which satisfies
\psi_{m}(1\otimes 1)=m
. More generally, for any
\omega\in{\bigwedge}^{r}M
(and
r\geq 0
) we let
\psi_{\omega}
be the element of
{\bigwedge}_{\mathcal{O}_{p}}^{r}\Hom_{G}(T_{p},M)
(resp. of
{\bigwedge}_{E_{\eta}}^{r}\Hom_{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}(W_{\eta},M)
) corresponding to
\omega
under the induced isomorphism on exterior products.

5.2.
Complex regulators

We first define a natural basis
\omega_{\infty}^{+}
of
\det_{E}\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{R},W)
in which we will compute all the complex regulators. The embedding
\iota_{\infty}:\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\subseteq\mathbb{C}
defines a place
w_{\infty}
(resp.
v_{\infty}
) of
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
(resp. of
k
) as well as a complex conjugation which is denoted by
\sigma_{\infty}
. As in §
4.1
, we denote by
V=\{v_{\infty,1},\ldots,v_{\infty,d^{+}}\}
the set of archimedean places of
k
which split completely in
L=H^{\ker\chi}
. We choose for each
i=1,\ldots,d^{+}
an automorphism
\tau_{\infty,i}\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
which sends
v_{\infty,i}
onto
v_{\infty}
and we put
w_{\infty,i}=\tau^{-1}_{\infty,i}(w_{\infty})
. For simplicity we still write
\tau_{\infty,i}
and
w_{\infty,i}
for their restrictions to finite extensions of
L
. We obtain a basis
\omega_{\infty}^{+}=\{t_{\infty,1},\ldots,t_{\infty,d^{+}}\}
of
\det_{E}H^{0}(\mathbb{R},W)=\det_{E}W^{\sigma_{\infty}=1}
by letting

t_{\infty,i}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\tau_{\infty,i}\otimes 1&\textrm{if }v_{i}\textrm{ is real,}\\
\tau_{\infty,i}\otimes 1+\sigma_{\infty}\cdot\tau_{\infty,i}\otimes 1&\textrm{if }v_{i}\textrm{ is complex.}\end{array}\right.

Note that it is moreover
T_{p}
-optimal for our fixed choice of
T_{p}
.

Lemma 5.2.1
.

Assume Conjecture
4.1.3
. Let
\eta\in\widehat{\Gamma}
be a character of order
p^{n}
. Put
\omega_{\mathrm{f},\eta}=\psi_{e_{\eta}\cdot\varepsilon_{n}^{\chi}}
if
\eta\neq\mathds{1}
and
\omega_{\mathrm{f},\eta}=\psi_{\xi^{\chi}}
if
\eta=\mathds{1}
(see Notation
5.1.2
). The complex regulator of
\rho\otimes\eta
computed in the bases
\omega_{\infty}^{+}
and
\omega_{\mathrm{f},\eta}
is equal to

\Reg_{\omega_{\infty}^{+}}(\rho\otimes\eta)=p^{-n\cdot d^{+}}\cdot L^{*}((\rho\otimes\eta)^{*},0).

Proof.

For
a\in L_{n}
which is seen in
L_{n,w_{\infty,i}}
, put

|a|_{w_{i}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\textrm{sgn}(a)a&\textrm{if }v_{i}\textrm{ is real,}\\
a\cdot\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mua\mkern-1.5mu}&\textrm{if }v_{i}\textrm{ is complex,}\end{array}\right.

where sgn is the sign function when
L_{n,w_{\infty,i}}=\mathbb{R}
and
a\mapsto\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mua\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
is the complex conjugation when
L_{n,w_{\infty,i}}=\mathbb{C}
. Write
e_{\eta}\cdot\varepsilon_{n}^{\chi}
(or
\xi^{\chi}
if
\eta
is trivial) as
\mu_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\mu_{d^{+}}
and write
